\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}={\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\widehat{\mathbf{\Psi}}({\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta),\quad\zeta\in\mathbb{C}\setminus(\mathbb{R}\cup\Gamma^{1}_{2}\cup\Gamma^{1}_{4}).

From the RHP for
\widehat{\mathbf{\Psi}}
we obtain the following properties for
\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
. First of all,
\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
is analytic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus(\mathbb{R}\cup\Gamma_{3}^{1}\cup\Gamma_{4}^{1})
. Along this discontinuity contour it possesses the jump relations

(8.30)

\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{+}(\zeta)=\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{-}(\zeta)\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}}(\zeta),\quad\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{cases}\mathbf{I}+\frac{1}{1+\ee^{\tau\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}\mathbf{E}_{12},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{1}^{0}=(1,+\infty),\\
\mathbf{I}+(1+\ee^{\tau\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})\mathbf{E}_{21},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{2}^{1}\cup\Gamma_{4}^{1},\\
\frac{1}{1+\ee^{\tau\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}\mathbf{E}_{12}-(1+\ee^{\tau\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})\mathbf{E}_{21},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{3}^{1}=(-\infty,1).\end{cases}

Note that this jump matrix coincides with the jump matrix (
8.27
) along
\mathbb{R}
, and along
\Gamma^{1}_{2}\cup\Gamma^{1}_{4}
this jump matrix appears as one of the factors in (
8.27
).

Next, RHP
7.4
–(3) yields the behavior,

(8.31)

\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)=\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}(\zeta^{-1})\right)\zeta^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{U}_{0}\ee^{-\frac{2}{3}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}\zeta^{3/2}},\quad\zeta\to\infty.

whereas RHP
7.4
–(4) gives that the estimate

(8.32)

\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)=\mathcal{O}(1),\quad\zeta\to 1,

is valid.

Finally, given any
R>0
, we can always choose
\varepsilon>0
to make sure that
|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta|\geq R
for every
\zeta\in\mathbb{C}
with
|\zeta|\geq\varepsilon
and for every
s
within the subcritical regime. In particular, we can ensure that
{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta
falls within the neighborhood at
\infty
where the expansion (
7.26
) is valid, and consequently we estimate

(8.33)

\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{\pm}(\zeta)=\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{1/2}}\right)\right)\zeta^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{U}_{0}\ee^{-\frac{2}{3}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}\zeta^{3/2}\bm{\sigma}_{3}},\quad\tau\to\infty,

uniformly for
\zeta\in\Gamma^{1}\setminus D_{\varepsilon}
, uniformly also for
s
within the subcritical regime.

As a final remark, the matrix
\widehat{\mathbf{\Psi}}
is bounded on bounded subsets of the plane: this follows from the fact that it is approximated by
\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}
(see for instance Theorem
7.9
). Thus, the rough estimate

\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)=\mathcal{O}(1+\zeta^{1/4})\ee^{-\frac{2}{3}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}\zeta^{3/2}\bm{\sigma}_{3}},\quad\tau\to\infty,

is also valid, now uniformly for
\zeta\in\mathbb{C}
, including for boundary values along
\Gamma^{1}
, and uniformly also for
s
within the subcritical regime.

8.2.3.
Conclusion of the asymptotic analysis in the subcritical regime

Moving towards the end of the asymptotic analysis in the subcritical regime, introduce

(8.34)

\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbf{\Phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{\tau}(\zeta)\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)^{-1},\quad\zeta\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma^{1},\quad\zeta\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma^{1}.

Set

\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Gamma^{1}\setminus\mathbb{R}.

Combining RHP
8.5
with the properties of
\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
, we obtain that
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
solves the following RHP.

RHP 8.6
.

Find a
2\times 2
matrix-valued function
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
with the following properties.

(1)

\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
is analytic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
.

(2)

The matrix
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
has continuous boundary values
\mathbf{R}_{\pm}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
along
\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
, and they are related by the jump relation
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{+}(\zeta)=\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{-}(\zeta)\mathbf{J}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)
,
\zeta\in\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
, with

(8.35)

\mathbf{J}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathbf{G}_{\pm}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12}\mathbf{G}_{\pm}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)^{-1}}{(1-\ee^{\mp\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})(1+\ee^{\tau\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})},\quad\zeta\in\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}\text{ with }\pm\re\zeta>0.

(3)

As
\zeta\to\infty
,

\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)=\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}(\zeta^{-1}).

(4)

As
\zeta\to 1
,

\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)=\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}(1),&\text{if }\ee^{\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(0)}\neq 1,\\
\mathcal{O}(1),&\text{if }\ee^{\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(0)}=1\text{ and }\zeta\notin\Omega_{1}^{1}\cup\Omega_{1}^{4},\\
\mathcal{O}\left((\zeta-1)^{-1}\right),&\text{if }\ee^{\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(0)}=1\text{ and }\zeta\in\Omega_{1}^{1}\cup\Omega_{1}^{4}.\end{cases}

The proof that
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
as we defined in (
8.34
) indeed solves the RHP
8.6
is as follows. The jump matrix for
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
is as usual computed from

\mathbf{J}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}=\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{-}\widehat{\mathbf{J}}_{\tau}(\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}})^{-1}(\mathbf{G}_{-}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})})^{-1}=\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{+}(\mathbf{J}_{\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}})^{-1}\widehat{\mathbf{J}}_{\tau}(\mathbf{G}_{+}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})})^{-1}.

With (
8.27
) and (
8.30
) in mind, expression (
8.35
) follows. Identities (
8.27
) and (
8.30
) also say that the jumps of
\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
and
\mathbf{\Phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{\tau}
coincide on
\mathbb{R}
, which explains why the obvious analyticity of
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
on
\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma^{1}
extends across
\mathbb{R}
as well, and RHP
8.6
–(1),(2) are established. Property RHP
8.6
–(3) follows from a simple combination of RHP
8.5
–(3) and (
8.31
), and RHP
8.6
–(4) is a consequence of RHP
8.5
–(4) and (
8.32
).

RHP
6.10
–(4) states that
\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau}
may have a pole behavior as
\zeta\to\lambda={\zeta_{\tau}(s)}
when
\ee^{\mathrm{i}\tau\mathsf{F}_{\tau}({\zeta_{\tau}(s)})}=1
. In the asymptotic analysis of the critical and supercritical regimes, this issue of possible pole behavior is sorted out on its own, once we construct a local parametrix. However, over here we do not construct a local parametrix, and this pole behavior remains, as reflected in RHP
8.6
. Even though one could prove that
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
is already close to the identity when
\ee^{\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(0)}\neq 1
, we want to handle both cases
\ee^{\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(0)}=1
and
\ee^{\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(0)}\neq 1
simultaneously, and for that we have to make another transformation, with the goal of sorting out such pole behavior.

As explained in the discussion following (
6.30
), the factor
1/(1+\ee^{\mathrm{i}\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)})
has poles accumulating from the upper and lower half planes towards
\lambda={\zeta_{\tau}(s)}
as
\tau\to\infty
. After the scaling
\zeta\mapsto{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta
, such poles are turned into poles of
1/(1+\ee^{\tau\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})
. These poles are determined by the equation
\tau\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)=(2k+1)\pi\mathrm{i},k\in\mathbb{Z}
, and thanks to (
6.8
), (
6.6
) and (
8.26
) they take the form

\zeta=1+\frac{1}{s}(2k+1)\pi\mathrm{i}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{s}{\tau^{3}}\right),\quad k\in\mathbb{Z}.

In particular, such expansion shows that
1/(1+\ee^{\tau\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}})
has no poles on any disk of the form
D_{\varepsilon s^{-1}}(1)
, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and any
s
within the subcritical regime.

Thus, we fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and transform

(8.36)

\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)=\begin{cases}\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12}\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)^{-1}}{(1-\ee^{-\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})(1+\ee^{\tau\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})}\right),&\zeta\in\Omega_{1}^{1}\cap D_{\varepsilon s^{-1}}(1),\\
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12}\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)^{-1}}{(1-\ee^{\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})(1+\ee^{\tau\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})}\right),&\zeta\in\Omega_{4}^{1}\cap D_{\varepsilon s^{-1}}(1),\\
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta),&\text{elsewhere}.\end{cases}

By our choice of
\varepsilon
, the factor
1/(1+\ee^{\tau\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}})
is analytic on the sectors
\Omega_{1}^{1}\cap D_{\varepsilon s^{-1}}(1)
and
\Omega_{4}^{1}\cap D_{\varepsilon s^{-1}}(1)
. Thanks to Proposition
6.2
–(ii), we also see that the factors
1/(1-\ee^{\pm\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}})
do not have poles on the corresponding sectors. Finally, recalling (
8.29
) we are sure that
\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
is analytic on the sectors
\Omega_{1}^{1}\cap D_{\varepsilon s^{-1}}(1)
and
\Omega_{4}^{1}\cap D_{\varepsilon s^{-1}}(1)
as well. Hence, the transformation (
8.36
) involves only analytic factors within
\Omega_{1}^{1}\cap D_{\varepsilon s^{-1}}(1)
and
\Omega_{4}^{1}\cap D_{\varepsilon s^{-1}}(1)
.

Set

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left(\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}\setminus D_{\varepsilon s^{-1}}(1)\right)\cup(\partial D_{\varepsilon s^{-1}}(1)\cap\Omega_{1}^{1})\cup(\partial D_{\varepsilon s^{-1}}(1)\cap\Omega_{4}^{1}),

with orientation as displayed in Figure
8
.

Figure 8
.
Geometric details for the transformation
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}\mapsto\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
. In solid, the contours that constitute the jump contour
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
.

Then
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
satisfies the following RHP.

RHP 8.7
.

Find a
2\times 2
matrix-valued function
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
with the following properties.

(1)

\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
is analytic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus\widehat{\Gamma}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
.

(2)

The matrix
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
has continuous boundary values
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{\pm}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
along
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
, and they are related by the jump relation
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{+}(\zeta)=\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{-}(\zeta)\widehat{\mathbf{J}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)
,
\zeta\in\widehat{\Gamma}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
, with

(8.37)

\widehat{\mathbf{J}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathbf{G}_{\pm}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12}\mathbf{G}_{\pm}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)^{-1}}{(1-\ee^{\mp\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})(1+\ee^{\tau\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})},\quad\zeta\in\widehat{\Gamma}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}\text{ with }\pm\re\zeta>0.

(3)

As
\zeta\to\infty
,

\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)=\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}(\zeta^{-1}).

(4)

\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)=\mathcal{O}(1)
as
\zeta\to 1
.

The verification of properties in
8.7
–(1),(3),(3) is standard as a direct consequence of the RHP
8.6
satisfied by
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
and the transformation (
8.36
).

The verification of
8.7
–(4) for
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}
as in (
8.36
) is a consequence of the following simple observation: the transformation (
8.36
) removes the jumps of
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
in a neighborhood of
\zeta=1
. Hence,
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}
has an isolated singularity at
\zeta=1
. Now using RHP
8.6
–(4) we see that this singularity cannot be essential, and evaluating the limit of
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
as
\zeta\to 1
along, say, the real axis, and using again RHP
8.6
–(4), we see that this singularity is in fact removable.

We are finally ready to establish the asymptotic estimates needed to conclude the asymptotic analysis.

Proposition 8.8
.

The estimate

\|\widehat{\mathbf{J}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}(\widehat{\Gamma}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})})}=\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}}\right)

holds true as
\tau\to\infty
, uniformly for
s
within the subcritical regime from Definition
7.1
.

Proof.

We use (
8.33
) and for
\zeta\in\widehat{\Gamma}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
we estimate as
\tau\to\infty
,

(8.38)

\frac{\mathbf{G}_{\pm}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{12}\mathbf{G}_{\pm}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)^{-1}}{(1-\ee^{-\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})(1+\ee^{\tau\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})}=\mathcal{O}(\zeta^{1/4})\frac{\ee^{\pm\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)-\frac{4}{3}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}\zeta^{3/2}}}{(1-\ee^{\pm\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})(1+\ee^{\tau\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})},\quad\pm\re\zeta>0,

where the error term is uniform for
\zeta\in\widehat{\Gamma}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
and
s
within the subcritical regime. To give estimates for the explicit scalar term on the right-hand side, we split into two parts, namely with
|\zeta-1|\leq\varepsilon
and
|\zeta-1|\geq\varepsilon
, where for convenience
\varepsilon
is the same used in the disk
D_{\varepsilon s^{-1}}(1)
in (
8.36
), which will eventually be chosen sufficiently small.

For
\zeta\in\widehat{\Gamma}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}\setminus D_{\varepsilon}(1)
, Proposition
6.9
–(ii),(iii) yield the uniform estimate

\ee^{\pm\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)-\frac{4}{3}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}\zeta^{3/2}}=\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-\tau{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\eta|\zeta-1|}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{1}{(1-\ee^{\pm\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})(1+\ee^{\tau\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})}=\mathcal{O}(1).

When combined with (
8.38
) and (
8.37
), these estimates are enough to show that

(8.39)

\|\widehat{\mathbf{J}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}(\widehat{\Gamma}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}\setminus D_{\varepsilon}(1))}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\tau{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}})=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta s}),

uniformly for
s
within the subcritical regime, where for the last identity we used that
{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}=\mathcal{O}(s/\tau)
, see (
6.6
).

For the estimate on the remaining part of the contour, that is, for
\zeta\in\widehat{\Gamma}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
with
\varepsilon s^{-1}\leq|\zeta-1|\leq\varepsilon
, we use again Proposition
6.9
–(ii), (iii) and now estimate

\ee^{\pm\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\tau{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\varepsilon/s})=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\varepsilon})\quad\text{and likewise}\quad\ee^{\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\varepsilon}).

Hence, by making
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small, we can make sure that
|1-\ee^{\pm\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}|\geq\delta,|1+\ee^{\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}|\geq\delta
for some
\delta>0
, and consequently

\frac{\ee^{\pm\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}{(1-\ee^{\pm\mathrm{i}\tau\widehat{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})(1+\ee^{\tau\widehat{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})}=\mathcal{O}(1),\quad\pm\re\zeta>0,

uniformly for
\zeta\in\widehat{\Gamma}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
with
\varepsilon s^{-1}\leq|\zeta-1|\leq\varepsilon
. On the other hand, by continuity we can choose
\varepsilon>0
such that

\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}\zeta^{3/2}}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}}),

uniformly for
|\zeta-1|\leq\varepsilon
. Combining these estimates, we obtain

(8.40)

\|\widehat{\mathbf{J}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{\infty}(\widehat{\Gamma}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}\cap D_{\varepsilon}(1))}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}}),

uniformly within the subcritical regime. The result is now completed combining (
8.39
) and (
8.40
), and observing that
{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}\ll s
for
s
in the subcritical regime.
∎

As a consequence, we obtain the estimates which were the final goal of the current section.

Theorem 8.9
.

The estimates

\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\widehat{\Gamma}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})})}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}}),\quad\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{\pm}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{2}(\widehat{\Gamma}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})})}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}})

as well as

\|\partial_{\zeta}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\widehat{\Gamma}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})})}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}}),\quad\|\partial_{\zeta}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{\pm}\|_{L^{2}(\widehat{\Gamma}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})})}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}}),

are valid as
\tau\to\infty
, uniformly for
s
within the subcritical regime from Definition
7.1
.

Proof.

The result is a consequence of Proposition
8.8
and the general small norm theory for RHPs.
∎

Since
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}=\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
along the real axis, we conclude

Corollary 8.10
.

The estimates

\|{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{\pm}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}})\quad\text{and}\quad\|\partial_{\zeta}{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{\pm}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}})

are valid as
\tau\to\infty
, uniformly for
s
within the subcritical regime from Definition
7.1
.

8.2.4.
Unwrap of the transformations in the subcritical regime

We unwrap the transformation
\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau}\mapsto\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}\mapsto\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}\mapsto\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
performed in Section
8.2
, arriving at the expression

(8.41)

\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}({\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta)={\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{-}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta){\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\widehat{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{-}({\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta)\quad\zeta\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{1\}.

Having in mind (
6.26
), (
8.41
) implies

(8.42)

{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\mathcal{H}_{\tau}({\zeta_{\tau}(s)}(\zeta-1))=\\
\left[{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}(\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\widehat{\mathbf{\Psi}})_{-}({\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta)\right]_{21}+\left[\widehat{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{-}({\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta)^{-1}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}(\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{-})(\zeta){\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\widehat{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{-}({\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta)\right]_{21},\quad\zeta>1.

By the conditions in RHP
8.7
, the only jumps of the function
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
on a neighborhood of
\zeta=1
are across the real axis. As a consequence,
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{-}
extends analytically from
\zeta>1
to
\zeta<1
. Also, the first term on the right-hand side coincides with
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}({\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta)
from (
7.27
), and therefore it also extends analytically from
\zeta>1
to
\zeta<1
. We just concluded that the identity

(8.43)

\mathcal{H}_{\tau}({\zeta_{\tau}(s)}(\zeta-1))=\widehat{\mathcal{H}}({\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta)+\frac{1}{{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}}\left[\widehat{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{-}({\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta)^{-1}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}(\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{-})(\zeta){\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\widehat{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{-}({\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta)\right]_{21}

holds true for
\zeta\in(-\kappa\tau^{2}/{\zeta_{\tau}(s)},\kappa\tau^{2}/{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})
, for some
\kappa>0
. We will use this identity later, together with Theorem
8.9
.

8.3.
Asymptotic analysis of the model problem in the supercritical regime

We now carry out the asymptotic analysis in the supercritical regime as stated in Definition
7.1
. We emphasize that under this regime,
{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}
satisfies the inequalities (
7.44
), which will be used extensively in the analysis that follows.

For the asymptotic analysis in the supercritical regime, we will first rescale
\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau}
appropriately, and from that on the analysis will follow more or less the same steps as the analysis of the regular regime, namely constructing local and global parametrices, and then applying the small norm theory. The main distinctions are that the global parametrix is rather explicit, given only in terms of algebraic and transcendental functions, and that the local parametrix has a similar non-constant-jump appeal as the model problem itself.

Now to the details.

8.3.1.
Scaling step

We first perform a scaling of the model problem,

\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathrm{i}}{4}\zeta_{\tau}(s)^{2}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau}\left(\zeta+\zeta_{\tau}(s)\right),\quad\zeta\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma^{0}.

The function
{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
satisfies a RHP which is obtained from a shift of the RHP for
\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau}
. For later convenience, we state this RHP explicitly. For its statement and in what follows, we set

(8.44)

\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathsf{P}_{\tau}\left(\zeta+\zeta_{\tau}(s)\right),\quad\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathsf{F}_{\tau}\left(\zeta+\zeta_{\tau}(s)\right),

RHP 8.11
.

Find a
2\times 2
matrix-valued function
{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
with the following properties.

(1)

{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
is analytic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma^{0}
.

(2)

The matrix
{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
has continuous boundary values
{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\tau,\pm}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
along
\Gamma^{0}\setminus\{0\}
, and they are related by
{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\tau,+}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)={\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\tau,-}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\tau}(\zeta)
,
\zeta\in\Gamma^{0}\setminus\{0\}
, where

(8.45)

\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\tau}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{cases}\mathbf{I}+\frac{1}{1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}\mathbf{E}_{12},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{0}^{0},\\
\mathbf{I}-\frac{1}{(1-\ee^{-\mathrm{i}\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})(1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})}\mathbf{E}_{12},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{1}^{0},\\
\left(\mathbf{I}+(1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{1}{(1-\ee^{-\mathrm{i}\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})(1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})}\mathbf{E}_{12}\right),&\zeta\in\Gamma_{2}^{0},\\
\frac{1}{1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}\mathbf{E}_{12}-(1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})\mathbf{E}_{21},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{3}^{0},\\
\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{1}{(1-\ee^{\mathrm{i}\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})(1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})}\mathbf{E}_{12}\right)\left(\mathbf{I}+(1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})\mathbf{E}_{21}\right),&\zeta\in\Gamma_{4}^{0},\\
\mathbf{I}-\frac{1}{(1-\ee^{\mathrm{i}\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})(1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})}\mathbf{E}_{12},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{5}^{0}.\end{cases}

(3)

As
\zeta\to\infty
,

(8.46)

{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)=\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}(\zeta^{-1})\right)\zeta^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{U}_{0}\ee^{-\left(\frac{2}{3}\zeta^{3/2}+\zeta_{\tau}(s)\zeta^{1/2}\right)\bm{\sigma}_{3}}.

(4)

As
\zeta\to 0
,

{\mathbf{\Phi}}_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)=\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}(1),&\text{if }\ee^{\mathrm{i}\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(0)}\neq 1,\\
\mathcal{O}(1),&\text{if }\ee^{\mathrm{i}\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(0)}=1\text{ and }\zeta\notin\Omega_{1}^{0}\cup\Omega_{4}^{0},\\
\mathcal{O}\begin{pmatrix}1&\zeta^{-1}\\
1&\zeta^{-1}\end{pmatrix},&\text{if }\ee^{\mathrm{i}\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(0)}=1\text{ and }\zeta\in\Omega_{1}^{0}\cup\Omega_{4}^{0}.\end{cases}

We now move on to the construction of global and local parametrices.

8.3.2.
Construction of the global parametrix

As we will see later on, the convergence

\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\tau}\to\begin{cases}\mathbf{E}_{12}-\mathbf{E}_{21},&\zeta\in\Gamma^{0}_{3},\\
\mathbf{I},&\text{ elsewhere in }\Gamma^{0},\end{cases}

holds true in the appropriate sense. Thus, the global parametrix
\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
should be the
2\times 2
matrix with this jump, and matching the leading asymptotics of
\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
as
\zeta\to\infty
. This lead us immediately to the choice

(8.47)

\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\zeta^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{U}_{0}\ee^{-\left(\frac{2}{3}\zeta^{3/2}+\zeta_{\tau}(s)\zeta^{1/2}\right)\bm{\sigma}_{3}},\quad\zeta\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma^{0}_{0}=\mathbb{C}\setminus(-\infty,0].

The matrix
\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
is analytic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus(-\infty,0]
, and for later we record the jump relation for it,

(8.48)

\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{+}(\zeta)=\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{-}(\zeta)(\mathbf{E}_{12}-\mathbf{E}_{21}),\quad\zeta\in(-\infty,0).

8.3.3.
The local parametrix

The rescaled model problem
\mathbf{\Phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
will be well-approximated by
\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
for values of
\zeta
away from the origin. Near the origin, we will need to construct a local parametrix.

Introduce

(8.49)

\mathbf{A}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\zeta^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{U}_{0}\ee^{-\frac{2}{3}\zeta^{3/2}\bm{\sigma}_{3}}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{-1}\zeta^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4},\quad\zeta\in\mathbb{C}.

Clearly
\mathbf{A}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
is analytic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus(-\infty,0]
. A standard calculation shows that
\mathbf{A}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
has no jump along
(-\infty,0]
, and that the (isolated) singularity at
\zeta=0
is removable. Hence, the matrix
\mathbf{A}
is in fact analytic on
\mathbb{C}
.

Recall that the matrix
\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{\tau}
is the solution to the RHP
7.11
. We fix
\varepsilon>0
and define the local parametrix needed as

(8.50)

\mathbf{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbf{A}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\tau^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{\tau}\left(w=\tau\zeta{\,|\,}y=-\frac{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}{\tau^{1/2}}\right),\quad\zeta\in D_{\varepsilon}\setminus\Gamma^{0}.

Thanks to Theorem
7.13
, and with (
7.44
) in mind, we know that this parametrix
\mathbf{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
exists. With the scaling
w=\tau\zeta
and the relation (
7.48
) in mind, it is immediate that

(8.51)

\mathbf{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{+}(\zeta)=\mathbf{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{-}(\zeta)\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\tau}(\zeta),\quad\zeta\in D_{\varepsilon}\cap\Gamma^{0},

with
\widetilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\tau}
as in (
8.45
). Finally, with the choice of
y
as in (
8.50
) and using RHP
7.11
–(3), it follows that the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{A}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\tau^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-1})\right)\tau^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\zeta^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{U}_{0}\ee^{-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta^{1/2}\bm{\sigma}_{3}}

(8.52)

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-1/2})\right)\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta),

as
\tau\to\infty
holds uniformly for
\zeta\in\partial D_{\varepsilon}
, for any
\varepsilon>0
fixed.

8.3.4.
Conclusion of the asymptotic analysis in the supercritical regime

To conclude the asymptotic analysis, we fix
\varepsilon>0
and transform

(8.53)

\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbf{\Phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\tau}(\zeta)\times\begin{cases}\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)^{-1},&\zeta\in\mathbb{C}\setminus(\Gamma^{0}\cup\overline{D}_{\varepsilon}),\\
\mathbf{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)^{-1},&\zeta\in D_{\varepsilon}\setminus\Gamma^{0}.\end{cases}

With

\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\Gamma^{0}\cup\partial D_{\varepsilon})\setminus D_{\varepsilon},

and with
\partial D_{\varepsilon}
oriented in the clockwise direction, this matrix
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
is the solution to the following RHP.

RHP 8.12
.

Find a
2\times 2
matrix-valued function
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
with the following properties.

(1)

\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
is analytic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
.

(2)

The matrix
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
has continuous boundary values
\mathbf{R}_{\pm}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
along
\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
, and they are related by the jump relation
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{+}(\zeta)=\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{-}(\zeta)\mathbf{J}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)
,
\zeta\in\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}
, with

(8.54)

\mathbf{J}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{cases}\mathbf{I}+\frac{\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}-2\zeta_{\tau}(s)\zeta^{1/2}}}{1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}\zeta^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{U}_{0}\mathbf{E}_{12}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{-1}\zeta^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{0}^{0}\setminus D_{\varepsilon},\\
\mathbf{I}-\frac{\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}-2\zeta_{\tau}(s)\zeta^{1/2}}}{(1-\ee^{-\mathrm{i}\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})(1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})}\zeta^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{U}_{0}\mathbf{E}_{12}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{-1}\zeta^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{1}^{0}\setminus D_{\varepsilon},\\
\begin{multlined}\left(\mathbf{I}+(1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})\ee^{\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}+2\zeta_{\tau}(s)\zeta^{1/2}}\zeta^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{U}_{0}\mathbf{E}_{21}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{-1}\zeta^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\right)\\
\times\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}-2\zeta_{\tau}(s)\zeta^{1/2}}}{(1-\ee^{-\mathrm{i}\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})(1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})}\zeta^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{U}_{0}\mathbf{E}_{12}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{-1}\zeta^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\right),\end{multlined}&\zeta\in\Gamma_{2}^{0}\setminus D_{\varepsilon},\end{cases}

and

(8.55)

\mathbf{J}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{cases}\mathbf{I}+\zeta_{-}^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{U}_{0}\left[\left(\frac{1}{1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}-1\right)\mathbf{E}_{11}+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}\mathbf{E}_{22}\right]\mathbf{U}_{0}^{-1}\zeta_{-}^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{3}^{0}\setminus D_{\varepsilon},\\
\begin{multlined}\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}-2\zeta_{\tau}(s)\zeta^{1/2}}}{(1-\ee^{\mathrm{i}\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})(1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})}\zeta^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{U}_{0}\mathbf{E}_{12}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{-1}\zeta^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\right)\\
\times\left(\mathbf{I}+(1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})\ee^{\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}+2\zeta_{\tau}(s)\zeta^{1/2}}\zeta^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{U}_{0}\mathbf{E}_{21}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{-1}\zeta^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\right),\end{multlined}&\zeta\in\Gamma_{4}^{0}\setminus D_{\varepsilon},\\
\mathbf{I}-\frac{\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}-2\zeta_{\tau}(s)\zeta^{1/2}}}{(1-\ee^{\mathrm{i}\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})(1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})}\zeta^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{U}_{0}\mathbf{E}_{12}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{-1}\zeta^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{5}^{0}\setminus D_{\varepsilon},\\
\mathbf{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)^{-1},&\zeta\in\partial D_{\varepsilon}.\end{cases}

(3)

As
\zeta\to\infty
,

\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)=\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}(\zeta^{-1}).

The verification that
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
as introduced in (
8.53
) indeed solves RHP
8.12
is as follows. The analyticity properties of
\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})},\mathbf{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
and
\mathbf{\Phi}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
imply that
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
has jumps on
\Gamma^{0}\cap\partial D_{\varepsilon}
. Thanks to the relations (
8.51
) and RHP
8.11
–(2), the matrix
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
has in fact no jumps on
(D_{\varepsilon}\cap\Gamma^{0})\setminus\{0\}
, and thus has an isolated singularity at
\zeta=0
. Sending
\zeta\to 0
along, say, the positive real axis, the conditions RHP
8.11
–(4) and RHP
7.11
–(4) show that this singularity is removable, concluding the verification of RHP
8.12
–(1).

The verification of RHP
8.12
–(2) is a straightforward calculation using (
8.47
), (
8.48
) and (
8.45
).

Finally, RHP
8.12
–(3) follows from (
8.46
) and (
8.47
).

To complete the asymptotic analysis we provide the convergence estimates for the jump matrix.

Proposition 8.13
.

The estimate

\left\|\mathbf{J}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}-\mathbf{I}\right\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}(\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})})}=\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-1/2})

holds true as
\tau\to\infty
, uniformly for
s
within the supercritical regime from Definition
7.1
.

Proof.

We start by recalling the relations (
8.44
) between
\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}
and
\mathsf{P}_{\tau}
, and between
\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}
and
\mathsf{F}_{\tau}
. These relations allow us to apply Proposition
6.9
in order to obtain estimates for terms involving
\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}}
and
\ee^{\pm\mathrm{i}\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}}
, and will be used in the sequel without further mention.

The decay

(8.56)

\mathbf{J}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}=\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-1/2})\quad\text{along}\quad\partial D_{\varepsilon}

is a direct consequence of the definition of
\mathbf{J}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
in (
8.55
) and the relation (
8.52
). We now show that in the remaining pieces of
\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
, the jump matrix
\mathbf{J}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
decays exponentially fast to the identity matrix in both
L^{1}
and
L^{\infty}
norms. The jumps on all of these components consist of a multiplication of scalar factors with matrix factors of the form

\zeta_{-}^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{U}_{0}\mathbf{E}_{ij}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{-1}\zeta_{-}^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}.

These matrix factors are roughly
\mathcal{O}(\zeta^{1/4})
in any component of
\Gamma_{0}\setminus\overline{D}_{\varepsilon}
, including in unbounded parts of these components. We will show in the rest of the proof that the scalar factors are all
\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\tau\eta|\zeta|})
for some constant
\eta>0
, uniformly for
\zeta\in\Gamma_{0}\setminus\overline{D}_{\varepsilon}
, including in unbounded components of this contour. Combining this exponential decay of the scalar parts with the algebraic decay of the matrix parts then yield that
{\mathbf{J}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
decay exponentially fast to the identity in
L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}(\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}\setminus\partial D_{\varepsilon})
, and when combined with (
8.56
) the proof is completed.

Proposition
6.9
provides the estimate
1/(1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\tau|\zeta|})
along
\Gamma_{0}^{0}\setminus D_{\varepsilon}=[\varepsilon,\infty)
, hence the exponential decay in
L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}
follows along this contour.

Along
\Gamma_{0}^{1}\setminus D_{\varepsilon}
, Proposition
6.9
–(i) yields
1/(1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\tau|\zeta|})
, and Proposition
6.9
–(iii) gives that

\frac{\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}-2{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta^{1/2}}}{1-\ee^{-\mathrm{i}\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}=\frac{\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}+\mathrm{i}\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)-2{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta^{1/2}}}{1-\ee^{\mathrm{i}\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}=\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-\tau\eta|\zeta|-2{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\re\zeta^{1/2}}\right).

Likewise, and having in mind that
\re(\zeta^{3/2})=0
and
\re\zeta^{1/2}=|\zeta|^{1/2}/2
for
\zeta\in\Gamma_{2}^{0}
, Proposition
6.9
–(ii) we obtain the estimate

(1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})\ee^{\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}+2{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta^{1/2}}=\mathcal{O}((1+\ee^{-\tau\eta|\zeta|})\ee^{{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|\zeta|^{1/2}}),\quad\zeta\in\Gamma_{2}^{0}\setminus D_{\varepsilon}.

As we are in the supercritical regime, we are certain that
{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}<0
for
\tau
sufficiently large, and moreover
{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}=\mathcal{O}(\tau^{1/2})
(see (
7.44
)). These last two displayed estimates show the claimed exponential convergence of the scalar factors along
(\Gamma_{1}^{0}\cup\Gamma_{2}^{0})\setminus D_{\varepsilon}(0)
. The analysis on
(\Gamma_{4}^{0}\cup\Gamma_{5}^{0})\setminus D_{\varepsilon}(0)
is similar.

At last, for the jump on
\Gamma_{3}^{0}\setminus D_{\varepsilon}
, Proposition
6.9
–(ii) implies that

\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\tau\eta|\zeta|})\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{1}{1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}-1=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\tau\eta|\zeta|}),

uniformly for
\zeta\in\Gamma_{0}^{3}=(-\infty,0)
. As we are only interested in values
\zeta\in\Gamma^{3}_{0}\setminus D_{\varepsilon}=(-\infty-\varepsilon]
, these estimates provide exponential decay of the scalar factors of
\mathbf{J}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
in
L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}
along
\Gamma_{3}^{0}\setminus D_{\varepsilon}
, concluding the proof.
∎

Thanks to Proposition
8.13
, we obtain

Theorem 8.14
.

The estimates

\|\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})})}=\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-1/2}),\quad\|\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\pm}-\mathbf{I}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})})}=\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-1/2})

as well as

\|\partial_{\zeta}\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})})}=\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-1/2}),\quad\|\partial_{\zeta}\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})})}=\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-1/2})

are valid as
\tau\to\infty
, uniformly for
s
within the supercritical regime.

Proof.

The proof is a direct consequence of Proposition
8.13
and the small norm theory for RHPs.
∎

8.3.5.
Unwrap of the transformations in the supercritical regime

We now unwrap the transformations in a form appropriate for what will be needed later.

For
\zeta
away from the origin, the unfolding of the transformations yields

(8.57)

\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})=\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathrm{i}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}}{4}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\mathbf{R}^{{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}}_{-}(\zeta)\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{-}(\zeta),\quad\zeta\in\mathbb{R}\setminus(-\delta,\delta).

Therefore

(8.58)

\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta)=\left[\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\right]_{21}+\left[\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{-}(\zeta)\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\right]_{21},\quad\delta<\zeta<\kappa\tau^{2},

as well as

(8.59)

\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta)=-\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\zeta}\left(\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}\right)-\left(1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}\right)^{2}\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}\right)\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}\right)\right]_{12}\\
-\left(1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}\right)^{2}\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}\right)\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{-}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{-}(\zeta)\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}\right)\right]_{12},

valid for
-\kappa\tau^{2}<\zeta<-\delta
, and where we recall that
\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)
was given in (
8.44
).

For
\zeta
near the origin, say
|\zeta|<\delta
, the unfolding gives

(8.60)

\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})=\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathrm{i}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}}{4}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\mathbf{R}^{{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}}(\zeta)\mathbf{A}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\tau^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{\tau,-}(w{\,|\,}y),\quad w=\tau\zeta,y=-\frac{{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}}{\tau^{1/2}}.

We look at this expression for
0<\zeta<\delta
. Recalling that the functions
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}
were introduced in (
6.26
) and (
7.49
), respectively, and the function
\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\scriptscriptstyle\rm mod})}}
from Proposition
7.12
, we obtain the identity

\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta{\,|\,}s)=\tau\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}(w{\,|\,}y)+\left[\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\scriptscriptstyle\rm mod})}}(w)^{-1}\tau^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\left(\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\mathbf{A}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\right)\tau^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\scriptscriptstyle\rm mod})}}(w)\right]_{21},

valid again for
0<\zeta<\delta
and with the identifications
w=\tau\zeta
and
y=-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}/\tau^{1/2}
. All the terms involved are analytic in a full neighborhood of the origin, so this identity extends to
-\delta<\zeta<\delta
as well. With (
7.50
) in mind, we just obtained

(8.61)

\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta{\,|\,}s)=\tau\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}(w{\,|\,}y)+\left[\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{\tau}(w)^{-1}\tau^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\left(\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\mathbf{A}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\right)\tau^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{\tau}(w)\right]_{21},\quad 0<\zeta<\delta,

and

(8.62)

\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta{\,|\,}s)=\tau\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}(w{\,|\,}y)+\Big[\left(\mathbf{I}+(1+\ee^{\mathcal{P}_{\tau}(w)})\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{\tau}(w)^{-1}\tau^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\\
\times\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\left(\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\mathbf{A}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\right)\tau^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{\tau}(w)\left(\mathbf{I}-(1+\ee^{\mathcal{P}_{\tau}(w)})\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\Big]_{21},

the latter valid for
-\delta<\zeta<0
, and where in these two identities we remind the reader that
w=\tau\zeta
,
\mathcal{P}_{\tau}=\mathcal{P}_{\tau}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)
is as in (
7.45
), and
\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{\tau}=\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{\tau}(\cdot{\,|\,}y)
with
y=-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}/\tau^{1/2}
.

9.
Consequences of the asymptotic analysis of the model problem

For later reference, we summarize the main findings that we already obtained along the way of the previous calculations, and that we will need later on.

We start with estimates on the model problem
\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau}
itself.

Proposition 9.1
.

The following estimates are valid.

(i)

For any
r>0
, the estimate

(9.1)

\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)=\mathcal{O}(1+|\zeta|^{1/4})\ee^{-(\frac{2}{3}\zeta_{-}^{3/2}+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta_{-}^{1/2})\bm{\sigma}_{3}}\\
\times\left[\mathbf{I}+\chi_{(-r,r)}(\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})\left(\mathsf{B}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}(\zeta)+\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\log\zeta\right)\mathbf{E}_{12}\right]\left(\mathbf{I}+\chi_{(-r,0)}(\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})(1+\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)})\mathbf{E}_{21}\right),

is valid as
\tau\to\infty
, uniformly for
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}
and uniformly for
s
within the critical regime, where we recall that
\mathsf{B}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}
is given in (
8.7
).

(ii)

For any
M>0
, the estimate

\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)=\mathcal{O}(1)(\chi_{\{|w|\leq M\}}(\zeta)+\chi_{\{|w|>M\}}(\zeta)\zeta_{-}^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4})\mathbf{U}_{0}\ee^{-\frac{2}{3}\zeta^{3/2}_{-}\bm{\sigma}_{3}}\left(\mathbf{I}+(1+\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)})\chi_{(0,{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{21}\right),

is valid uniformly for
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}
and uniformly for
s
within the subcritical regime.

(iii)

For any
\delta>0
,
\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}
has the following estimates valid as
\tau\to\infty
, uniformly for
s
within the supercritical regime:

(a)

For
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}
with
|\zeta|>\delta
,

\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})=\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathrm{i}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}}{4}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\mathcal{O}(1)\zeta^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{U}_{0}\ee^{-(\frac{2}{3}\zeta_{-}^{3/2}+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta_{-}^{1/2})\bm{\sigma}_{3}}.

(b)

For
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}
with
\delta/{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{2}\leq|\zeta|\leq\delta
,

\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})=\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathrm{i}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}}{4}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\mathcal{O}(1)|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{s}{\tau^{2}}\right)\right)\mathbf{B}_{-}({\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{2}\zeta),

where
\mathbf{B}
is the Bessel parametrix from RHP
7.15
.

(c)

For
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}
with
|\zeta|\leq\delta/{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{2}
,

\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})=\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathrm{i}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}}{4}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\mathcal{O}(1)|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{s}{\tau^{2}}\right)\right)\mathbf{B}_{-}({\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{2}\zeta)\mathbf{L}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon},-}({\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{2}\zeta),

where again
\mathbf{B}
is the Bessel parametrix from RHP
7.15
, and
\mathbf{L}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}
is given in (
7.68
).

All the estimates above may be differentiated term by term.

Proof.

The estimate (i) was already proven in (
8.21
).

For (ii), recall that the unwrap
\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau}\mapsto\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}\mapsto\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}\mapsto\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
yielded (
8.41
), and we further work on it. First, observe that
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{-}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
may be estimated from Corollary
8.10
. Moving further and also unfolding the transformations
\widehat{\mathbf{\Psi}}\mapsto\widehat{\mathbf{\Psi}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{({\scriptscriptstyle\rm mod})}}\mapsto\widehat{\mathbf{R}}
from (
7.29
) and (
7.34
), and estimating
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}
using Theorem
7.9
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}\left({\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{1/2}\ee^{-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}}\right)\right)\widehat{{\mathbf{Ai}}}_{-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}+(1+\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)})\chi_{(0,{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(1)\widehat{{\mathbf{Ai}}}_{-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}+(1+\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)})\chi_{(0,{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{21}\right).

The very definitions of
\widehat{{\mathbf{Ai}}}
in (
7.32
) and of
{\mathbf{Ai}}
in (
C.2
) show that
\widehat{{\mathbf{Ai}}}_{-}(\zeta)={\mathbf{Ai}}_{-}(\zeta)
for
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}
. Having in mind that
{\mathbf{Ai}}_{-}
is bounded on compacts and its asymptotics as
\zeta\to\infty
is as in (
C.4
), part (ii) follows.

Part (iii)–(a) is a direct consequence of (
8.57
) combined with the uniform boundedness for
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
provided by Theorem
8.14
and the explicit expression for
\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
in (
8.47
).

For both (iii)-(b) and (iii)-(c), our starting point is (
8.60
). Using Theorem
8.14
to bound
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
therein, and also that
\mathbf{A}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
is analytic near the origin and independent of
\tau
(see (
8.49
)), we simplify (
8.60
) to

\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})=\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathrm{i}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}}{4}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\mathcal{O}(1)\tau^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{\tau,-}(\tau\zeta{\,|\,}y=-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}/\tau^{2}),

valid for
|\zeta|\leq\delta
, for any
\delta>0
fixed. In the regime in (iii)-(b), asymptotics for
\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{\tau,-}
are now provided by
7.78
and Theorem
7.19
, whereas in the regime in (iii)–(c) asymptotics for
\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{\tau,-}
are concluded from (
7.79
) and Theorem
7.19
.
∎

Next, we collect estimates for the function
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}
from (
6.26
). As we will show later, these estimates are at the core of the proof of Theorem
2.8
.

Theorem 9.2
.

Fix
\kappa>0
sufficiently small. The following estimates are valid.

(i)

For any
\eta\in(0,1)
sufficiently small, the estimate

\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}{\,|\,}s)=2\pi\mathrm{i}\,\mathsf{A}(\zeta,\zeta)+\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-\eta{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}-\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}_{+}}\right),

holds true as
\tau\to\infty
, uniformly for
\zeta\in[-\kappa\tau^{2},\kappa\tau^{2}]
and uniformly for
s
within the subcritical regime, where
\mathsf{A}
is the Airy kernel (
1.2
).

(ii)

For any
L>0
there exists
\eta>0
such that the estimates

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}{\,|\,}s)=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta(\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})}),\qquad L<\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}<\kappa\tau^{2},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}{\,|\,}s)=\mathcal{O}(|\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|),\qquad-\kappa\tau^{2}<\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}<-L,

and

\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}{\,|\,}s)=2\pi\mathrm{i}\,\mathsf{K}_{\rm P34}(\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)},\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}{\,|\,}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})+\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-1}),\quad|\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|\leq L,

are valid as
\tau\to\infty
, uniformly for
s
within the critical regime, and uniformly for
\zeta
in each of the corresponding sets, and where
\mathsf{K}_{\rm P34}
is the Painlevé XXXIV kernel from (
7.21
)–(
7.22
).

(iii)

For any
\delta>0
sufficiently small, there exists
\eta>0
such that the estimates

\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}{\,|\,}s)\frac{\ee^{\tau{\mathsf{P}}(\zeta{\,|\,}s)}}{(1+\ee^{\tau{\mathsf{P}}(\zeta{\,|\,}s)})^{2}}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\tau|\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|}),\quad\zeta\in[-\kappa\tau^{2},\kappa\tau^{2}]\setminus[{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}-\delta,{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}+\delta],

and

\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta{\,|\,}s)=-2\pi\mathrm{i}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{2}\mathsf{J}_{0}(-\xi,-\xi)\\
+\mathcal{O}\left((1+|\xi|^{1/2})\ee^{2\xi_{+}^{1/2}}\left(|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|+|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|^{4}\tau^{-1}\right)\right),\quad\xi={\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{2}\zeta,\quad|\zeta|\leq\delta,

are valid as
\tau\to\infty
, uniformly for
s
within the supercritical, where
\mathsf{J}_{0}
is the Bessel kernel from (
2.57
).

Each of the estimates above may be differentiated term by term.

Proof.

For the claim (i), our starting point is (
8.43
). Theorem
8.9
ensures that
\mathbf{\Delta}_{w}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{-}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(w)=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}})
uniformly on the interval of integration above. Then, using the asymptotics (
7.26
) we are sure that the rough estimate

(9.2)

\left[\widehat{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{-}({\zeta_{\tau}(s)}w)^{-1}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}(\mathbf{\Delta}_{w}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{-})(w){\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\widehat{\mathbf{\Psi}}_{-}({\zeta_{\tau}(s)}w)\right]_{21}=\\
\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}}\ee^{-4{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}w^{3/2}/3}(1+|w|^{1/2}){\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{1/2}\right),&w\geq 0,\\
\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}}(1+|w|^{1/2}){\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{1/2}\right),&w\leq 0,\end{cases}

is valid uniformly for
w=\mathcal{O}(\tau^{2}/{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})
. Setting
\zeta={\zeta_{\tau}(s)}w
and using this estimate in (
8.43
), we obtain

\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})=\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(\zeta)+E_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta),\quad\tau\to\infty,

where the error term
E_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
satisfies

E_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)=\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}}\ee^{-4\zeta^{3/2}/3}({\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{-1/2}+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{-1}|\zeta|^{1/2})\right),&0\leq\zeta\leq\kappa\tau^{2},\\
\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{3/2}}({\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{-1/2}+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{-1}|\zeta|^{1/2})\right),&-\kappa\tau^{2}\leq\zeta\leq 0,\end{cases}

valid as
\tau\to\infty
uniformly for
s
in the subcritical regime.

We now express
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}
in terms of the Airy kernel. Recalling once again (
7.27
) and using (
7.29
) and (
7.34
), we write

(9.3)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(\zeta{\,|\,}s)

\displaystyle=\left[\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)\right]_{21,-}+\left[\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}(\zeta)\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)\right]_{21,-}

(9.4)

\displaystyle=\left[\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}{\mathbf{Ai}}_{0}(\zeta)\right]_{21,-}+\left[\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}(\zeta)\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)\right]_{21,-},\quad\zeta>{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}.

By RHP
7.7
, the definition of
{\mathbf{Ai}}_{0}
in (
C.1
) and Proposition
7.5
, every term in this identity is analytic on the interval
(0,+\infty)
, so this identity extends to
\zeta>0
. The term
\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}
can be analytically continued to
\zeta<0
using (
7.32
), and its analytic continuation is

\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{E}_{21}).

Thanks to Proposition
7.10
, the analytic continuation of
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{-}
from
\zeta>0
to
\zeta<0
is

\widehat{\mathbf{R}}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}+\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)}\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)\mathbf{E}_{21}\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)^{-1}\right).

Using these last two expressions, we analytically continue the right-hand side of (
9.4
) from
\zeta>0
to
\zeta<0
. As a result, after a cumbersome but straightforward calculation we obtain the identity

(9.5)

\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(\zeta{\,|\,}s)=\\
\begin{cases}\left[\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}{\mathbf{Ai}}_{0}(\zeta)\right]_{21,-}+\left[\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}(\zeta)\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)\right]_{21,-},&\zeta>0,\\
\begin{aligned} &\left[\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}{\mathbf{Ai}}_{0}(\zeta)\right]_{21,-}-\ee^{2\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)^{-1}\partial_{\zeta}\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)\right)_{21,-}\\
&+\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)}\left[(\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{E}_{21})[\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)^{-1}\partial_{\zeta}\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta),\mathbf{E}_{21}](\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{E}_{21})\right]_{21,-}\\
&+\left[(\mathbf{I}+(1-\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)})\mathbf{E}_{21})\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}(\zeta)\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)(\mathbf{I}-(1-\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)})\mathbf{E}_{21})\right]_{21,-},\end{aligned}&-\kappa\tau^{2}<\zeta<0,\end{cases}

which is valid for some
\kappa>0
.

We now estimate the terms on the right-hand side above. From asymptotics of Airy functions and Theorem
7.9
, we see that

\left|\left[\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}(\zeta)\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)\right]_{21,-}\right|=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\ee^{-4\zeta^{3/2}/3}),\quad\zeta>0,\quad\tau\to\infty,

for some
\eta>0
. Next, using the fact that in the subcritical regime we are considering here we have
{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}>0
, and thus
|\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|=|\zeta|+|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|
for
\zeta\leq 0
, we obtain again from Proposition
6.9
that

\left|\ee^{2\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)^{-1}\partial_{\zeta}\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)\right)_{21,-}\right|\\
+\left|\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)}\left[(\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{E}_{21})[\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)^{-1}\partial_{\zeta}\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta),\mathbf{E}_{21}](\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{E}_{21})\right]_{21,-}\right|=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\tau{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}}),\quad\tau\to\infty.

Also, using again asymptotics of Airy functions, we obtain for
\zeta\leq 0
,

\left[(\mathbf{I}+(1-\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)})\mathbf{E}_{21})\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}(\zeta)\widehat{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)(\mathbf{I}-(1-\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)})\mathbf{E}_{21})\right]_{21,-}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})).

All in all, we conclude that

\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})=\left[\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}{\mathbf{Ai}}_{0}(\zeta)\right]_{21,-}+\widehat{E}_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta),\quad\tau\to\infty,

where this new error term satisfies

\widehat{E}_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(\zeta)=\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}}\ee^{-\eta{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\right),&0\leq\zeta\leq\kappa\tau^{2},\\
\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-\eta{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\right),&-\kappa\tau^{2}\leq\zeta\leq 0.\end{cases}

The result now follows reducing
\eta
, and also using (
C.1
) and (
1.2
).

Next, we move to part (ii), taking as a starting point (
6.27
).

The case
\zeta>{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}+L
is a direct consequence of (
8.23
), and we now focus on
\zeta<{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}-L
.

To bound the term involving
\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}
in (
6.27
) we use (
8.23
), and also the fact that
\zeta_{-}^{3/2}\in\mathrm{i}\mathbb{R}
for
\zeta<0
. As a consequence, for
\zeta<{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}-L
we update (
6.27
) to

\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})=-\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}^{\prime}(\zeta)\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)}-(1+\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)})^{2}\mathcal{O}(\zeta).

Equation (
6.17
) ensures that
\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)}\leq M\ee^{-\eta\tau|\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|}
, uniformly in
s
within the critical regime. Moreover, a direct calculation shows that

\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}^{\prime}(\zeta)=\tau\mathsf{P}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\zeta}{\tau^{2}}\right).

By making
\kappa>0
sufficiently small, we can make sure that whenever
-\kappa\tau^{2}<\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}<-L
, the quotient
\zeta/\tau^{2}
falls within the disk of analyticity of
\mathsf{P}
(see Definition
6.1
), and therefore
\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}^{\prime}(\zeta)=\mathcal{O}(\tau)
uniformly for
\zeta\in J
as
\tau\to\infty
and
s
in the critical regime. The second claim in (ii) then follows.

Next, we treat the third estimate in (ii). Combining the expression of
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}
from the first line of (
6.27
) with (
8.24
), we obtain the identity

(9.6)

\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta)=\mathcal{H}(\zeta{\,|\,}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})+\left[\mathbf{\Psi}_{0}(\zeta{\,|\,}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}(\zeta+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})\mathbf{\Psi}_{0}(\zeta{\,|\,}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})\right]_{21},

which is an identity initially valid for
\zeta\in(0,L)
, with
L
sufficiently small. However, all the terms involved in this identity are analytic near
\zeta=0
: the analyticity of the left-hand side is ensured by Proposition
6.11
, the analyticity of
\mathcal{H}
is ensured by Proposition
7.3
, the shift of
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}
is analytic by RHP
8.2
–(1) (observe that
\Gamma^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}
does not intersect a neighborhood of
{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}
), and
\mathbf{\Psi}_{0}
is analytic by RHP
7.2
–(4). Thus, the identity above extends analytically to a full neighborhood of the origin. The
L^{\infty}
estimates for
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
and its
\zeta
-derivative from Theorem
8.4
then ensure that

(9.7)

\left[\mathbf{\Psi}_{0}(\zeta{\,|\,}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}(\zeta+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})\mathbf{\Psi}_{0}(\zeta{\,|\,}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})\right]_{21}=\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-1}),\quad\tau\to\infty,

uniformly for
\zeta\in[-L,L]
and uniformly for
s
within the critical regime, and the result now follows using (
7.22
).

We now move to the proof of (iii). Set

J\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=J^{+}\cup J^{-},\quad J^{+}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\delta,\kappa\tau^{2}],\quad J^{-}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=[-\kappa\tau^{2},-\delta).

We first analyze the case
\zeta\in J^{+}
. As a starting point, we use the representation (
8.58
). An explicit calculation from (
8.47
) yields

(9.8)

\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)=-\left(\zeta^{1/2}+\frac{{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}}{2\zeta^{1/2}}\right)\bm{\sigma}_{3}+\frac{1}{4\zeta}\ee^{\left(\frac{2}{3}\zeta^{3/2}+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta^{1/2}\right)\bm{\sigma}_{3}}\bm{\sigma}_{2}\ee^{-\left(\frac{2}{3}\zeta^{3/2}+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta^{1/2}\right)\bm{\sigma}_{3}},\quad\zeta\in\mathbb{C}\setminus(-\infty,0].

Hence,

\left[\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\right]_{21}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}-2{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta^{1/2}}),\quad\zeta\in J^{+},

and in a similar manner, in combination with Theorem
8.14
,

\left[\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\mathbf{R}_{-}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\right]_{21}=\mathcal{O}(\tau^{-1/2}\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}-2{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta^{1/2}}),\quad\zeta\in J^{+}.

With (
8.58
) in mind and using Proposition
6.9
–(i), we obtain

\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta)\frac{\ee^{\tau{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}{\,|\,}s)}}{\big(1+\ee^{\tau{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}{\,|\,}s)}\big)^{2}}=\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}-2{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta^{1/2}-\eta\tau\zeta}\right),\quad\zeta\in J^{+},

where the error term is uniform for
\zeta\in J^{+}
and
s
within the supercritical regime.

Having in mind that
{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}<0
and
{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}=\mathcal{O}(s/\tau)
so that
{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}/\tau=\mathit{o}(1)
, we express

-2{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}\zeta^{1/2}-\eta\tau\zeta=\tau\zeta\left(\frac{2|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|}{\tau\zeta^{1/2}}-\eta\right)\leq-\frac{\eta}{2}\tau\zeta,\quad\zeta\in J^{+},

from which we conclude that for a new constant
\eta>0
,

(9.9)

\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta{\,|\,}s)\frac{\ee^{\tau{\mathsf{P}}(\zeta+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}{\,|\,}s)}}{(1+\ee^{\tau{\mathsf{P}}(\zeta+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}{\,|\,}s)})^{2}}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\tau\zeta}),\quad\tau\to\infty,\quad\zeta\in J^{+},

where the error term is uniform in
\zeta
, and also uniform for
s
within the supercritical regime.

Next, for the estimate for
\zeta\in J^{-}
, we start with the representation (
8.59
) for
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}
. A simple estimate using Proposition
6.9
–(ii) yields

(9.10)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\zeta}\left(\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}\right)\frac{\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}{(1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)})^{2}}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\tau|\zeta|}),\quad\tau\in J^{-},

for some
\eta>0
, uniformly for
s
within the supercritical regime.

Next, from (
9.8
) and again Proposition
6.9
–(ii) we compute the rough estimate

\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}\right)\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}\right)\right]_{12}=\mathcal{O}(\zeta^{1/2}),\quad\zeta\in J^{-},

and then again applying Proposition
6.9
–(ii),

(9.11)

\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}\right)\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}\right)\right]_{12}\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\tau|\zeta|}),\quad\tau\to\infty,\quad\zeta\in J^{-},

uniformly for
s
within the supercritical regime.
Finally, using now (
8.47
) and Theorem
8.14
, we get the rough bound

\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}\right)\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{-}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{-}(\zeta)\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}\right)\right]_{12}=\mathcal{O}(\zeta^{1/2}\tau^{-1/2}),

and then once again

(9.12)

\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}\right)\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{-}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{-}(\zeta)\mathbf{G}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}\right)\right]_{12}\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta)}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\tau|\zeta|}),\quad\zeta\in J^{-},

as
\tau\to\infty
, uniformly for
s
within the supercritical regime.

Recalling (
8.59
), we summarize (
9.9
), (
9.10
), (
9.11
), (
9.12
) into the estimate

(9.13)

{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}(\zeta)\frac{\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}(\zeta{\,|\,}s)}}{(1+\ee^{\tau\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}(\zeta{\,|\,}s)})^{2}}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\tau|\zeta|}),\quad\tau\to\infty,\quad\zeta\in J,

uniformly for
s
within the supercritical regime. Recalling that
\widetilde{\mathsf{P}}_{\tau}(\zeta+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)})=\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)
(see (
8.44
)), this last estimate is the same as the claim in (iii) with
\delta=L
.

Finally, we move on to the proof of the second estimate in (iii). The starting points for it are equations (
8.61
) and (
8.62
). Recall that in Section
7.3.1
we performed the change of variables
\xi=y^{2}w
in the RHP for
\mathbf{Y}_{\tau}(\cdot{\,|\,}y)
, and in Equation (
8.50
) we later identified
w=\tau\zeta
and
y=-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}/\tau^{1/2}
. Unfolding these two transformations, we identify
\zeta=\xi/(\tau y^{2})=\xi/{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{2}
. In the formulas that will follow, we use
\xi,w,\zeta
as variables for simplicity, and they always correspond to the just mentioned correspondences.

We first re-express (
8.61
) using (
7.78
), (
7.79
) and (
7.80
), which leads to

(9.14)

\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta{\,|\,}s)={\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{2}[\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}\mathbf{B}(\xi)]_{21}+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{2}\left[\mathbf{B}_{0}(\xi)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}(\xi)\mathbf{B}_{0}(\xi)\right]_{21}\\
+\left[\mathbf{B}(\xi)^{-1}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}(\xi)^{-1}|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\left(\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\mathbf{A}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\right)|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}(\xi)\mathbf{B}(\xi)\right]_{21,-},\quad 0<\zeta<\delta.

In the expression above, we already accounted for the explicit expression for
\mathbf{L}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}
in (
7.68
).

The function
\mathbf{A}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
is analytic in a neighborhood of the origin and independent of
\tau
(see (
8.49
)), and therefore it remains bounded as
\tau\to\infty
. The functions
\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}(\xi)
and
\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)
both decay uniformly to the identity matrix (see Theorem
8.14
and Theorem
7.19
) and therefore

\left[\mathbf{B}(\xi)^{-1}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}(\xi)^{-1}|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\left(\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\mathbf{A}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\right)|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}(\xi)\mathbf{B}(\xi)\right]_{21,-}\\
=\left[\mathbf{B}(\xi)^{-1}\mathcal{O}(1)|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\mathcal{O}(1)|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\mathcal{O}(1)\mathbf{B}(\xi)\right]_{21,-}=\left[\mathbf{B}(\xi)^{-1}\mathcal{O}(|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|)\mathbf{B}(\xi)\right]_{21,-}

We are interested in values
0<\zeta<\delta
, which correspond to
0<\xi<\delta{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{2}
, hence also possibly growing with
\tau
. For bounded values of
\xi>0
, the expansion in RHP
7.15
–(4) ensures that

\left[\mathbf{B}(\xi)^{-1}\mathcal{O}(|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|)\mathbf{B}(\xi)\right]_{21,-}=\left[\mathbf{B}_{0}(\xi)^{-1}\mathcal{O}(|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|)\mathbf{B}_{0}(\xi)\right]_{21}=\mathcal{O}(|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|).

For unbounded values of
\xi>0
, we use RHP
7.15
–(3) instead, and obtain that

\left[\mathbf{B}(\xi)^{-1}\mathcal{O}(|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|)\mathbf{B}(\xi)\right]_{21,-}=\left[\ee^{-\xi^{1/2}\bm{\sigma}_{3}}\mathbf{U}_{0}^{-1}\xi^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathcal{O}(|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|)\xi^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{U}_{0}\ee^{\xi^{1/2}\bm{\sigma}_{3}}\right]_{21}=\mathcal{O}(|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|\xi^{1/2}\ee^{2\xi^{1/2}}).

All in all, these bounds lead to

\left[\mathbf{B}(\xi)^{-1}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}(\xi)^{-1}|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\left(\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\mathbf{A}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\right)|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}(\xi)\mathbf{B}(\xi)\right]_{21,-}\\
=\mathcal{O}((1+|\xi|^{1/2})\ee^{2\xi^{1/2}}|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|),

valid as
\tau\to\infty
, uniformly for
0<\zeta<\delta
and uniformly for
s
within the supercritical regime.
In a completely analogous way, we obtain

[\mathbf{B}_{0}(\xi)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{Y}}(\xi)\mathbf{B}_{0}(\xi)]_{21}=\mathcal{O}((1+|\xi|^{1/2})\ee^{2\xi^{1/2}}|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|^{2}\tau^{-1}),\quad\tau\to\infty,

where the term
{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{2}\tau^{-1}=y^{2}
comes from Theorem
7.19
, and we emphasize that the error term is uniform for
0<\zeta<\delta
and also uniform for
s
within the supercritical regime. Recalling that
{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}=\mathcal{O}(s\tau^{-1})
and
s
is within the supercritical regime, and using also the identity (
7.65
), we update (
9.14
) to

(9.15)

\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta{\,|\,}s)=-2\pi\mathrm{i}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{2}\mathsf{J}_{0}(-\xi,-\xi)+\mathcal{O}\left((1+|\xi|^{1/2})\ee^{2\xi^{1/2}}(|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|+|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|^{4}\tau^{-1})\right),\quad\tau\to\infty,

uniformly for
0<\zeta<\delta
and uniformly for
s
within the supercritical regime. Observe that
|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|^{4}/\tau\leq C|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|
for
\tau\leq|s|\leq\tau^{4/3}
, whereas
C|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|^{4}/\tau\geq|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|
for
\tau^{4/3}\leq|s|\leq\tau^{3/2}
, but for now it suffices to keep the error term as presented above. This estimate concludes the second estimate in (iii) for when
\zeta\geq 0
.

The analysis for
-\delta<\zeta<0
is similar. We re-express (
8.62
) using again (
7.78
), (
7.79
) and (
7.80
), as well as (
7.65
) and the definition of
\mathbf{L}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}
in (
7.68
), which now leads to

(9.16)

\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta{\,|\,}s)=-2\pi\mathrm{i}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{2}\mathsf{J}_{0}(-\xi,-\xi)+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{2}[\mathbf{B}_{0}(\xi)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}(\xi)\mathbf{B}_{0}(\xi)]_{21,-}\\
+\left[(\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{E}_{21})\mathbf{B}(\xi)^{-1}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}(\xi)^{-1}|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}(\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\mathbf{A}^{{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}}(\zeta))|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}(\xi)\mathbf{B}(\xi)(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{E}_{21})\right]_{21,-},

valid for
-\varepsilon<\xi<0
, corresponding to
-\varepsilon/{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{2}<\zeta<0
, as well as

(9.17)

\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta{\,|\,}s)=-2\pi\mathrm{i}{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{2}\mathsf{J}_{0}(-\xi,-\xi)+{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{2}[\mathbf{B}_{0}(\xi)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\xi}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}(\xi)\mathbf{B}_{0}(\xi)]_{21,-}+\Big[(\mathbf{I}+(1+\ee^{\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}(\xi)})\mathbf{E}_{21})\mathbf{B}(\xi)^{-1}\\
\times\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}(\xi)^{-1}|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}(\mathbf{R}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(\zeta)\mathbf{A}^{{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}}(\zeta))|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\mathbf{R}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}(\xi)\mathbf{B}(\xi)(\mathbf{I}-(1+\ee^{\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}(\xi)})\mathbf{E}_{21})\Big]_{21,-},

which is valid for
-\delta{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{2}<\xi<-\varepsilon
, that is,
-\delta<\zeta<-\varepsilon/{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}^{2}
. For this last identity, we recall that
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}
was introduced in (
7.53
).
