, and the asymptotic formulas obtained from the RHP analysis of Section
11
can be employed.

Later on, we will fix
S
within any of the regimes of Assumptions
2.7
, and look at many of the quantities coming next as functions of
s
with
S
fixed, but with both
s
and
S
within the same regime from Assumptions
2.7
. For that reason, it is convenient to denote many of the quantities below as functions of
s
only, and think of
S
as fixed along the way. For the same reason, we refer to many of the upcoming estimates as being valid uniformly in
s
, without referring to the uniformity in
S
. We stress anyway that
S
will always be kept as a fixed value satisfying (
12.1
).

We use (
10.5
) and decompose

(12.2)

\log\frac{\mathsf{Z}_{N}^{\sigma}(S)}{\mathsf{Z}^{\sigma}_{N}(s)}=\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}{\Omega^{\sigma}}(z{\,|\,}v)\mathbf{e}_{2}^{T}\mathbf{X}(x{\,|\,}v)^{-1}\mathbf{X}^{\prime}(x{\,|\,}v)\mathbf{e}_{1}\;\mathrm{d}v=\mathsf{I}_{1}(S,s)+\mathsf{I}_{2}(S,s)+\mathsf{I}_{3}(S,s),

with

(12.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{1}=\mathcal{X}_{1}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\cap(0,z_{N}(s)),\quad\mathcal{X}_{2}=\mathcal{X}_{2}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\cap[z_{N}(s),\mathsf{b}),\quad\mathcal{X}_{3}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\cap[\mathsf{b},+\infty),

\displaystyle\text{and}

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}_{j}(S,s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{j}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}{\Omega^{\sigma}}(z{\,|\,}v)\mathbf{e}_{2}^{T}\mathbf{X}(x{\,|\,}v)^{-1}\mathbf{X}^{\prime}(x{\,|\,}v)\mathbf{e}_{1}\;\mathrm{d}v,\quad j=1,2,3.

The sets
\mathcal{X}_{1}=\mathcal{X}_{1}(v)
and
\mathcal{X}_{2}=\mathcal{X}_{2}(v)
depend on the variable of integration but
\mathcal{X}_{3}
does not, and in the definition of
\mathsf{I}_{3}
we convention that
\mathcal{X}_{3}(v)=\mathcal{X}_{3}=\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\cap[\mathsf{b},+\infty)
. By making
\delta>0
sufficiently small, we can always assume that each set
\mathcal{X}_{j}(v)
is non-empty, for every
v\in[s,S]
.

Although the matrix
\mathbf{X}
has poles at the points of summation, the term
\mathbf{e}_{2}^{T}\mathbf{X}(x)^{-1}\mathbf{X}^{\prime}(x)\mathbf{e}_{1}
involves only entries which are entire functions. In particular, we can replace their values by the boundary value
\mathbf{e}_{2}^{T}\mathbf{X}_{+}(x)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{+}^{\prime}(x)\mathbf{e}_{1}
.

We split the coming analysis of the
\mathsf{I}_{j}
’s into several parts, corresponding to the split of the terms in the sum in
x
in the intervals
(0,z_{N}(s)),[z_{N}(s),\mathsf{b})
and
[\mathsf{b},\infty)
. In each of these intervals, we unfold the transformations
\mathbf{X}\mapsto\mathbf{Y}\mapsto\mathbf{T}\mapsto\mathbf{S}
. This will yield new expressions for
\mathsf{I}_{j}
, decomposing them into new integrals involving
\mathbf{S}
, and other explicit factors that can be integrated in
v
exactly, so that they give rise to sums. After that, we split our analysis once again in two separate parts, one of them corresponding to the estimate of the
v
-integrated sums, and the remaining part that depends on
\mathbf{S}
.

In the analysis of the terms involving
\mathbf{S}
we unwrap once more
\mathbf{S}\mapsto\mathbf{R}
, which now depends on whether we are inside or outside the local lenses where local parametrices have been constructed.

For the coming calculations, we recall the notation
\mathbf{\Delta}=\mathbf{\Delta}_{z}
which is introduced in (
2.53
) and will be used extensively in what follows.

12.1.
Unwrapping
\mathbf{X}\mapsto\mathbf{S}

We undo the transformations
\mathbf{X}\mapsto\mathbf{Y}\mapsto\mathbf{T}\mapsto\mathbf{S}
. We do it in each of the intervals
(0,z_{N}(s)),(z_{N}(s),\mathsf{b})
and
(\mathsf{b},\infty)
in separate subsections below.

12.1.1.
Contributions around the saturated interval

For
x\in(0,z_{N}(s))\setminus\mathcal{X}_{1}
, we obtain

\mathbf{X}_{+}(x{\,|\,}s)=\ee^{(N\ell-\mathsf{C})\bm{\sigma}_{3}}(-2\pi\mathrm{i}N)^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\mathbf{S}_{+}(x{\,|\,}s)\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\ee^{2N\phi_{+}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}}{(1-\ee^{2\pi\mathrm{i}Nx})\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\\
\times(-2\pi\mathrm{i}N)^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\Pi_{N}(x)^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}}\ee^{(-N\phi(x)+\pi\mathrm{i}Nz+N\mathsf{V}(z)/2+\mathsf{C})\bm{\sigma}_{3}},

and therefore for
x
in the same set

(12.4)

\mathbf{e}_{2}^{T}\mathbf{X}_{+}(x{\,|\,}s)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{+}^{\prime}(x{\,|\,}s)\mathbf{e}_{1}=-2\pi\mathrm{i}N\Pi_{N}(x)^{2}\ee^{-2N\phi_{+}(x)+2\pi\mathrm{i}Nz+N\mathsf{V}(x)+2\mathsf{C}}\\
\times\mathbf{e}_{2}^{T}\left[\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\ee^{2N\phi_{+}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}}{(1-\ee^{2\pi\mathrm{i}Nx})\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\mathbf{S}_{+}(x{\,|\,}s)^{-1}\right]\left[\mathbf{S}_{+}(x{\,|\,}s)\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\ee^{2N\phi_{+}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}}{(1-\ee^{2\pi\mathrm{i}Nx})\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]^{\prime}\mathbf{e}_{1}.

The expression in the second line of (
12.4
) may be brought to the form

(12.5)

-\left(\frac{\ee^{2N\phi_{+}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}}{(1-\ee^{2\pi\mathrm{i}Nx})\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)^{2}\left[\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{S}_{+}(x{\,|\,}s)\right]_{12}+\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}N\ee^{2\pi\mathrm{i}Nx+2N\phi_{+}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}}{(1-\ee^{2\pi\mathrm{i}Nx})^{2}\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}+\frac{1}{1-\ee^{2\pi\mathrm{i}Nx}}\times(\ast)+(\ast),

where
\ast
represent different terms involving
\sigma,\widetilde{\phi}_{+},\mathsf{E}
and the entries of
\mathbf{S}_{+}^{-1}
and
\mathbf{S}_{+}^{\prime}
. In particular, these terms are all non-singular along the real axis. The factor
1/(1-\ee^{2\pi\mathrm{i}N})
has simple poles at points in
\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, while
\Pi_{N}^{2}
has double zeros at the same points. Therefore, when we evaluate the right-hand side of (
12.4
) in points
x\in\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, the terms singled out explicitly in (
12.5
) are the only ones that do not vanish, and after some further straightforward simplification we are left with

(12.6)

\mathbf{e}_{2}^{T}\mathbf{X}_{+}(x{\,|\,}s)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{+}^{\prime}(x{\,|\,}s)\mathbf{e}_{1}=-\frac{1}{{\Omega^{\sigma}}(x{\,|\,}s)}+\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\frac{\ee^{2N\phi_{+}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}}{{\Omega^{\sigma}}(x{\,|\,}s)\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)}\left[\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{S}_{+}(x{\,|\,}s)\right]_{12},

which is valid for
x\in(\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0})\cap(0,z_{N}(s))
. We thus obtained the exact formulation

\mathsf{I}_{1}(S,s)=-\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\;\mathrm{d}v+\mathsf{J}_{1}(S,s),

with

(12.7)

\mathsf{J}_{1}(S,s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}\;\ee^{2N\phi_{+}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}\left[\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{S}_{+}(x{\,|\,}v)\right]_{12}\mathrm{d}v.

From (
11.19
) we see that
\ee^{2N\widetilde{\phi}_{+}(x)}=\ee^{-2N\widetilde{\phi}_{+}(x)}
for
x\in\mathcal{X}_{j}(v)
, for any
j
, so we can (and will) interchange between
\phi_{+}
and
\widetilde{\phi}_{+}
in the sum above and other sums that will follow.

12.1.2.
Contributions around the bulk interval

For
x\in(z_{N}(s),\mathsf{b})
we obtain

\mathbf{X}_{+}(x{\,|\,}s)=(-2\pi\mathrm{i}N)^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\ee^{(N\ell-\mathsf{C})\bm{\sigma}_{3}}\mathbf{S}_{+}(x{\,|\,}s)\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\ee^{2N\phi_{+}(x)}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)\ee^{\mathsf{E}(x)}}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\\
\times(-2\pi\mathrm{i}N)^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\ee^{(-2N\phi_{+}(x)+N\mathsf{V}(x)+\mathsf{C})\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{{\Omega^{\sigma}}(x{\,|\,}v)}{\Pi_{N}(x)}\ee^{\pi\mathrm{i}Nx}\mathbf{E}_{12}\right)

Observe that the term
\Pi_{N}
generates poles at the points
x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}(s)
, so we should actually interpret this identity to be valid for
x\in(z_{N}(s),\mathsf{b})\setminus\mathcal{X}_{2}(s)
.

After cumbersome but straightforward calculations, from this expression we obtain

(12.8)

\mathbf{e}_{2}^{T}\mathbf{X}_{+}(x{\,|\,}s)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{+}^{\prime}(x{\,|\,}s)\mathbf{e}_{1}=-\frac{\ee^{N\mathsf{V}(x)+2\mathsf{C}-\mathsf{E}(x)}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)}\left(2N\phi^{\prime}(x)-\mathsf{E}^{\prime}(x)-\frac{\sigma^{\prime}(x{\,|\,}s)}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)}\right)\\
-\frac{\ee^{-2N\phi_{+}(x)+N\mathsf{V}(x)+2\mathsf{C}}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\left[\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\ee^{2N\phi_{+}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{S}_{+}(x{\,|\,}s)\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\ee^{2N\phi_{+}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]_{21}.

In principle, this identity is valid for
x\in(z_{N}(s),\mathsf{b})\setminus\mathcal{X}_{2}(s)
. However, in this process of taking the
(2,1)
-entry the poles coming from
(1-\ee^{-2\pi\mathrm{i}Nx})
disappeared, and the right-hand side is regular on the whole interval
(z_{N}(s),\mathsf{b})
. Thus, by continuity this identity extends from
(z_{N}(s),\mathsf{b})\setminus\mathcal{X}_{2}(s)
to the whole interval
[z_{N}(s),\mathsf{b})
.

Using this identity back in (
12.3
), we obtain

(12.9)

\mathsf{I}_{2}(S,s)=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\left(2N\phi_{+}^{\prime}(x)-\frac{\sigma^{\prime}(x{\,|\,}v)}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}-\mathsf{E}^{\prime}(x)\right)\mathrm{d}v+\mathsf{J}_{2}(S,s),

with

(12.10)

\mathsf{J}_{2}(S,s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\\
\times\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\ee^{2N\phi_{+}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}}\left[\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\ee^{2N\phi_{+}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{S}_{+}(x{\,|\,}v)\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\ee^{2N\phi_{+}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]_{21}\mathrm{d}v.

12.1.3.
Contributions away from the support of the equilibrium measure

We now consider the unwrap of the transformations
\mathbf{X}\mapsto\mathbf{Y}\mapsto\mathbf{T}\mapsto\mathbf{S}
on the interval
(\mathsf{b},\infty)
. The resulting formula is

\mathbf{X}_{+}(x{\,|\,}s)=\ee^{(N\ell-\mathsf{C})\bm{\sigma}_{3}}(-2\pi\mathrm{i}N)^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\mathbf{S}_{+}(x)\\
\times\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)\ee^{-2N\phi_{+}(x)+\mathsf{E}(x)}}{1-\ee^{-2\pi\mathrm{i}Nx}}\mathbf{E}_{12}\right)\ee^{(-N\phi_{+}(x)+N\mathsf{V}(x)/2+\mathsf{C})\bm{\sigma}_{3}}(-2\pi\mathrm{i}N)^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}.

As before, both the left-hand and right-hand sides have poles at points in
\mathcal{X}_{3}
, so this identity should be interpreted as being valid for
x\in(\mathsf{b},\infty)\setminus\mathcal{X}_{3}
.

Using this identity, a cumbersome but straightforward calculation yields that

\left[\mathbf{X}_{+}(x{\,|\,}s)^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{+}^{\prime}(x{\,|\,}s)\right]_{21}=-\frac{\ee^{-2N\phi_{+}(x)+N\mathsf{V}(x)+2\mathsf{C}}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\left[\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{S}_{+}(x{\,|\,}s)\right]_{21}.

Similarly as before, by continuity we see that this identity is valid not only in
(\mathsf{b},\infty)\setminus\mathcal{X}_{3}
but in the whole interval
[\mathsf{b},\infty)
. Returning this identity to (
12.3
) we obtain

(12.11)

\mathsf{I}_{3}(S,s)=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{3}}\left(\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1\right)\ee^{-2N\phi_{+}(x)+\mathsf{E}(x)}\left[\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{S}_{+}(x{\,|\,}v)\right]_{21}\mathrm{d}v=\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}\mathsf{J}_{3}(S,s).

The introduction of the new notation
\mathsf{J}_{3}
is made just for notational convenience in the next steps.

12.1.4.
Summary of the result of the unwrap of
\mathbf{X}\mapsto\mathbf{S}

Summarizing, from (
12.2
) we obtained the representation

(12.12)

\log\frac{\mathsf{Z}_{N}^{\sigma}(S)}{\mathsf{Z}^{\sigma}_{N}(s)}=\mathsf{J}_{1}(S,s)+\mathsf{J}_{2}(S,s)+\mathsf{J}_{3}(S,s)-\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\;\mathrm{d}v,\\
-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\left(2N\phi_{+}^{\prime}(x)-\frac{\sigma^{\prime}(x{\,|\,}v)}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}-\mathsf{E}^{\prime}(x)\right)\mathrm{d}v,

with

(12.13)

\displaystyle\mathsf{J}_{1}(S,s)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}\;\ee^{2N\phi_{+}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}\left[\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{S}_{+}(x{\,|\,}v)\right]_{12}\mathrm{d}v.

\displaystyle\mathsf{J}_{2}(S,s)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\begin{multlined}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\ee^{2N\phi_{+}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}}\\
\times\left[\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\ee^{2N\phi_{+}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{S}_{+}(x{\,|\,}v)\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\ee^{2N\phi_{+}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]_{21}\mathrm{d}v\end{multlined}

\displaystyle\mathsf{J}_{3}(S,s)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{3}}\left(\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1\right)\ee^{-2N\phi_{+}(x)+\mathsf{E}(x)}\left[\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{S}_{+}(x{\,|\,}v)\right]_{21}\mathrm{d}v

12.2.
Unwrap of the last transformation

Expressions (
12.12
)–(
12.13
) now involve the matrix
\mathbf{S}
, and now we unwrap the transformation
\mathbf{S}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
from (
11.34
). Inside
U_{\mathsf{a}}
and
U_{\mathsf{b}}
, the unwrapping yields

(12.14)

\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{S}_{+}(x)=\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{P}_{+}(x)+\mathbf{P}(x)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{R}(x)\mathbf{P}(x),

whereas outside these neighborhoods, the unwrapping gives

(12.15)

\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{S}_{+}(x)=\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{G}_{+}(x)+\mathbf{G}(x)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{R}(x)\mathbf{G}(x).

To continue, let us assume without loss of generality that
U_{\mathsf{a}}\cap\mathbb{R}=(\mathsf{a}-\epsilon,\mathsf{a}+\epsilon)
,
U_{N}\cap\mathbb{R}=(\mathsf{b}-\epsilon,\mathsf{b}+\epsilon)
, for some
\epsilon>0
which is independent of
N,s,t
.
We have to explore formulas (
12.14
)–(
12.15
) separately, depending on whether
x
is in each of the components

(12.16)

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathcal{X}_{1}(s)\setminus\overline{U}_{\mathsf{a}}=\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\cap(0,\mathsf{a}-\epsilon),\quad

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathcal{X}_{1}(s)\cap\overline{U}_{\mathsf{a}}=\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\cap[\mathsf{a}-\epsilon,z_{N}(s)],

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathcal{X}_{2}(s)\cap U_{\mathsf{a}}=\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\cap(z_{N}(s),\mathsf{a}+\epsilon),\quad

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathcal{X}_{2}(s)\setminus U_{\mathsf{a}}=\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\cap[\mathsf{a}+\epsilon,\mathsf{b}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathcal{X}_{3}(s)\cap U_{\mathsf{b}}=\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\cap[\mathsf{b},\mathsf{b}+\epsilon),\quad

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathcal{X}_{3}(s)\setminus U_{\mathsf{b}}=\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\cap[\mathsf{b}+\epsilon,+\infty).

We emphasize that the sets
\mathcal{X}_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
are independent of
s,t
, and hence of
v
. This is so because the neighborhoods
U_{\mathsf{a}}
and
U_{\mathsf{b}}
are independent of
s,t
. We observe also that
\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
involves nodes both outside and inside
U_{\mathsf{b}}
, but such distinct nature of points will not play any major role. In line with such split, we also split the integrals in (
12.13
) into

(12.17)

\mathsf{J}_{k}(S,s)=\mathsf{J}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{J}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}(S,s),\quad k=1,2,3,

where the upper index
{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
corresponds to the integral whose integrand sums over
x\in\mathcal{X}_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)
and the index
{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
corresponds to the integral whose integrand sums over
x\in\mathcal{X}_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}(v)
.

As we will see, the major contributions will come from
\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(s)
and
\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(s)
, which correspond to the node points falling inside
U_{\mathsf{a}}
. So in the next sections, we first account for such contributions, and then express the right-hand side (
12.12
) in a different way, to single out the terms that will turn out to either be relevant or asymptotic negligible.

12.2.1.
Contributions near
\mathsf{a}

Let us focus now on rewriting
\mathsf{J}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
and
\mathsf{J}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
, which correspond to the terms involving nodes in
U_{\mathsf{a}}
, where we will use (
12.14
). Recalling (
11.23
), near
x=\mathsf{a}
it is convenient to use the local coordinate

\zeta=\zeta(x)=N^{2/3}\varphi(x).

Still thanks to (
11.23
), we recall that the map
z\mapsto\zeta
sends the
+
-boundary value of
\mathbb{R}
near
\mathsf{a}
to the
-
-boundary value of
\mathbb{R}
near the origin. We emphasize that in such a case

(12.18)

\zeta^{\prime}=\frac{\mathrm{d}\zeta}{\mathrm{d}z}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{M}(\zeta)=\frac{\mathrm{d}\zeta}{\mathrm{d}z}\mathbf{M}(\zeta)^{-1}\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbf{M}}{\mathrm{d}\zeta}(\zeta)=\zeta^{\prime}\mathbf{\Delta}_{\zeta}\mathbf{M}(\zeta),

see for instance (
2.54
).

From the expression (
11.29
) we obtain

\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{P}_{+}(x)=-\left(\frac{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}(x)}{2}+N\widetilde{\phi}_{+}^{\prime}(x)\right)\bm{\sigma}_{3}+\ee^{(N\widetilde{\phi}_{+}(x)+\pi\mathrm{i}/2+\mathsf{E}(x)/2)\bm{\sigma}_{3}}\bm{\sigma}_{1}\\
\times\left[\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)+\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathbf{\bm{\sigma}}_{1}\Delta\mathbf{A}(x)\bm{\sigma}_{1}\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\right]\bm{\sigma}_{1}\ee^{-(N\widetilde{\phi}_{+}(x)+\pi\mathrm{i}/2+\mathsf{E}(x)/2)\bm{\sigma}_{3}}

In particular, a simple calculation shows that

(12.19)

\left[\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{P}_{+}(x)\right]_{12}=-\ee^{2N\widetilde{\phi}_{+}(x)+\mathsf{E}(x)}\left[\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)+\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)^{-1}\bm{\sigma}_{1}\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{A}(x)\bm{\sigma}_{1}\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta))\right]_{21}.

Exploring the relation (
11.19
) and the fact that
2N\pi\mathrm{i}x\in 2\pi\mathbb{Z}
for
x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}
, a more cumbersome but still straightforward calculation yields

(12.20)

\left[\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\ee^{2N\phi_{+}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{P}_{+}(x)\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\ee^{2N\phi_{+}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]_{21}=\\
\begin{aligned} &\frac{2N\widetilde{\phi}_{+}^{\prime}(x)+\mathsf{E}^{\prime}(x)}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\ee^{2N\phi_{+}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}-\ee^{-2N\widetilde{\phi}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\right]_{12}\\
&-\ee^{-2N\widetilde{\phi}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)^{-1}\bm{\sigma}_{1}\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{A}(x)\bm{\sigma}_{1}\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\right]_{12}.\end{aligned}

These identities are valid for any
x\in\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\cap U_{\mathsf{a}}
.

From (
12.13
), (
12.19
) and the explicit expression for
\mathbf{P}
from (
11.29
) we thus split

\mathsf{J}^{{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}}_{1}(S,s)=\mathsf{S}_{1}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{1,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{1,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s),

with

(12.21)

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}_{1}(S,s)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}\left[\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\right]_{21}\mathrm{d}v,

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{1,1}^{{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}}(S,s)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}\left[\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)^{-1}\bm{\sigma}_{1}\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{A}(x)\bm{\sigma}_{1}\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\right]_{21}\mathrm{d}v,

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{1,2}^{{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}}(S,s)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}\left[\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)^{-1}\bm{\sigma}_{1}\mathbf{A}(x)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{R}(x)\mathbf{A}(x)\bm{\sigma}_{1}\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\right]_{21}\mathrm{d}v,

where we used that
\widetilde{\phi}
is analytic on
(0,\mathsf{a})
, and therefore
\widetilde{\phi}_{+}=\widetilde{\phi}
in this interval. We stress that
\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau}=\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau}(\cdot{\,|\,}v)
as well as all the other matrix functions above depend on the integration variable
v
, but in the above and in what follows we omit this dependence on matrix-valued functions, to lighten notation.

Likewise, we also obtain from (
12.6
), (
12.14
) and (
12.19
),

(12.22)

\mathbf{e}_{2}^{T}\mathbf{X}(x{\,|\,}s)^{-1}\mathbf{X}^{\prime}(x{\,|\,}s)\mathbf{e}_{1}=-\frac{1}{{\Omega^{\sigma}}(x{\,|\,}s)}-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\frac{1}{{\Omega^{\sigma}}(x{\,|\,}s)\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)}\Big\{\left[\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\right]_{21}\\
+\left[\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)^{-1}\bm{\sigma}_{1}\left(\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{A}(x)+\mathbf{A}(x)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{R}(x)\mathbf{A}(x)\right)\bm{\sigma}_{1}\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\right]_{21}\Big\},\quad x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}.

In a similar manner, using (
12.10
) and (
12.20
) we split

(12.23)

\mathsf{J}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\left(2N\widetilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{+}(x)+\mathsf{E}^{\prime}(x)\right)\mathrm{d}v+\mathsf{S}_{2}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{2,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s),

where

(12.24)

\mathsf{S}_{2}(S,s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}\left(\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1\right)\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\right]_{12}\mathrm{d}v

and

(12.25)

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{multlined}\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}\left(\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1\right)\\
\times\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)^{-1}\bm{\sigma}_{1}\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{A}(x)\bm{\sigma}_{1}\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\right]_{12}\mathrm{d}v\end{multlined}

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{2,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{multlined}\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}\left(\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1\right)\\
\times\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)^{-1}\bm{\sigma}_{1}\mathbf{A}(x)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{R}(x)\mathbf{A}(x)\bm{\sigma}_{1}\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\right]_{12}\mathrm{d}v,\end{multlined}

as well as

(12.26)

\mathbf{e}_{2}^{T}\mathbf{X}(x{\,|\,}s)^{-1}\mathbf{X}^{\prime}(x{\,|\,}s)\mathbf{e}_{1}=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\frac{1}{{\Omega^{\sigma}}(x{\,|\,}s)}\left(\frac{\sigma^{\prime}(x{\,|\,}s)}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)}-2\pi\mathrm{i}\right)-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\frac{1}{\Omega(x{\,|\,}s)}\Bigg[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)}\right)\\
\times\Big(\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)+\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)^{-1}\bm{\sigma}_{1}\left(\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{A}(x)+\mathbf{A}(x)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{R}(x)\mathbf{A}(x)\right)\bm{\sigma}_{1}\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\Big)\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)}\right)\Bigg]_{12},

valid for
x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
.

12.2.2.
Summary of the contributions

The identity (
12.12
) now updates to

(12.27)

\log\frac{\mathsf{Z}_{N}^{\sigma}(S)}{\mathsf{Z}^{\sigma}_{N}(s)}=\mathsf{S}_{1}(S,s)+\mathsf{S}_{2}(S,s)\\
-\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\;\mathrm{d}v-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\left(2N\widetilde{\phi}^{\prime}_{+}(x)+\mathsf{E}^{\prime}(x)\right)\mathrm{d}v\\
-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\left(2N\phi_{+}^{\prime}(x)-\frac{\sigma^{\prime}(x{\,|\,}v)}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}-\mathsf{E}^{\prime}(x)\right)\mathrm{d}v\\
+\mathsf{J}_{1}^{{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}}+\mathsf{J}_{2}^{{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}}+\mathsf{J}_{3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}+\mathsf{J}_{3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}+\mathsf{K}_{1,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}+\mathsf{K}_{1,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}+\mathsf{K}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}+\mathsf{K}_{2,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})},

where we stress that in the above
\mathsf{J}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}=\mathsf{J}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}(S,s)
,
\mathsf{J}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}=\mathsf{J}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)
,
\mathsf{K}_{j,k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}=\mathsf{K}_{j,k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)
.

Next, and as a last step before the asymptotic analysis of each of the terms above, we do some rewriting of some of the explicit terms, as well as of the sum
\mathsf{S}_{1}+\mathsf{S}_{2}
.

Thanks to the relation between
\phi
and
\widetilde{\phi}
in (
11.19
), the sums over
\mathcal{X}_{2}
and
\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
in (
12.27
) combine into

(12.28)

2\pi\mathrm{i}N\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}-\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}(v)}\frac{(\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1)\sigma^{\prime}(x{\,|\,}v)}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}\\
+\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\left(2N\phi_{+}^{\prime}(x)-\mathsf{E}^{\prime}(x)\right).

When plugging this expression back into (
12.27
), the sum above over
\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)
combines with the sum over
\mathcal{X}_{1}(v)
of (
12.27
), yielding a final sum over
\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\cap(0,\mathsf{a}+\epsilon)
, where
\mathsf{a}+\epsilon
is the point of intersection of
\partial U_{\mathsf{a}}
with
\mathbb{R}
which lies to the right of
\mathsf{a}
. Observe now that this set is independent of
v
. The sum runs over finitely many terms, and we interchange it with the integration, performing it explicitly. As a result we obtain

\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\cap(0,\mathsf{a}+\epsilon)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\mathrm{d}v=\sum_{x\in\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\cap(0,\mathsf{a}+\epsilon)}\log\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}S)}.

With

(12.29)

\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{a}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\cap(0,\mathsf{a}+\epsilon),

which is independent of
v
, our formula (
12.27
) is now updated to

(12.30)

\log\frac{\mathsf{Z}_{N}^{\sigma}(S)}{\mathsf{Z}^{\sigma}_{N}(s)}=\mathsf{S}_{1}(S,s)+\mathsf{S}_{2}(S,s)+\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}(v)}\frac{(\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1)\sigma^{\prime}(x{\,|\,}v)}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}\mathrm{d}v-\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{a}}}\log\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}S)}\\
-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\left(2N\phi_{+}^{\prime}(x)-\mathsf{E}^{\prime}(x)\right)\mathrm{d}v+\mathsf{R}(S,s),

where we set

(12.31)

\mathsf{R}=\mathsf{R}(S,s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathsf{J}_{1}^{{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}}(S,s)+\mathsf{J}_{2}^{{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}}(S,s)+\mathsf{J}_{3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}(S,s)+\mathsf{J}_{3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)\\
+\mathsf{K}_{1,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{1,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{2,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s).

To conclude this section, we still need to work out the sum
\mathsf{S}_{1}+\mathsf{S}_{2}
a little more. For that, we recall the precise form
\sigma(x)=1+\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)}
(see (
11.25
)), which is valid for
z\in U_{\mathsf{a}}
, and the function
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}
introduced in (
6.26
). Using Proposition
6.11
and the relation in (
12.18
), we obtain

(12.32)

\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}\left[\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\right]_{21}=\zeta^{\prime}\frac{\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}{(1+\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)})^{2}}\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta-\zeta_{\tau}(v){\,|\,}v),\quad x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})},

and

(12.33)

(\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1)\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\right]_{12}=\\
-\zeta^{\prime}\frac{\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}{(1+\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)})^{2}}\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta-\zeta_{\tau}(v){\,|\,}v)-\frac{(\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1)\sigma^{\prime}(x{\,|\,}v)}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}},\quad x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}.

Thus, from the very definition of
\mathsf{S}_{1}
and
\mathsf{S}_{2}
in (
12.21
) and (
12.24
), we obtain

(12.34)

\mathsf{S}_{1}(S,s)+\mathsf{S}_{2}(S,s)=\mathsf{S}(S,s)-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}_{2}(v)}\frac{(\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1)\sigma^{\prime}(x{\,|\,}v)}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}\mathrm{d}v,

where, setting

(12.35)

\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)\cup\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)=\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\cap U_{\mathsf{a}}=\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\cap(\mathsf{a}-\epsilon,\mathsf{a}+\epsilon),

which is independent of
v
, the term
\mathsf{S}
is

(12.36)

\mathsf{S}(S,s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}}\zeta^{\prime}\frac{\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)}}{(1+\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)})^{2}}\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\zeta-\zeta_{\tau}(v))\mathrm{d}v.

We emphasize that both
\mathsf{P}_{\tau}=\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta{\,|\,}v)
and
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}=\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(\cdot{\,|\,}v)
depend on the variable of integration
v
. When we plug (
12.34
) into (
12.30
), the explicit sum in (
12.34
) cancels the terms on
\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)
of the first explicit sum in (
12.30
), and we obtain the final expression

(12.37)

\log\frac{\mathsf{Z}_{N}^{\sigma}(S)}{\mathsf{Z}^{\sigma}_{N}(s)}=\mathsf{S}(S,s)-\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{a}}}\log\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}S)}\\
+\mathsf{R}(S,s)-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\left(2N\phi_{+}^{\prime}(x)-\mathsf{E}^{\prime}(x)-\frac{\sigma^{\prime}(x{\,|\,}v)}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\mathrm{d}v.

This estimate concludes the calculations of the current section. Our next and final task is to analyze
\mathsf{S}(s)
asymptotically as
N\to\infty
.

13.
Asymptotic analysis of the multiplicative statistics

In this section, we carry out the asymptotic analysis of the right-hand side of (
12.37
). Such term consists of three main parts, namely the term
\mathsf{R}
, the term
\mathsf{S}
and the remaining explicit terms. This explicit term will turn out to consist of relevant terms near
z=\mathsf{a}
plus asymptotically negligible terms; this is proven in Section
13.1
. Next, in Section
13.2
we provide estimates that will show that
\mathsf{R}
is asymptotically negligible as well. Such estimates will depend on each of the regimes from Assumptions
2.7
, and heavily rely on the estimates provided by Proposition
9.1
. Finally, in Section
13.3
we perform the asymptotic analysis of
\mathsf{S}
. It is in this step that the Poisson summation formula plays a role. The analysis itself also depends on which regime of
s
we are, and it heavily relies on Theorem
9.2
.

13.1.
Analysis of the explicit terms

On the right-hand side of (
12.37
) there are two explicit terms, one involving a sum over the set
\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{a}}
which was defined in (
12.29
), and the remaining term which is a sum over
\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
. As we will see later, the first term is not asymptotically negligible. But the second term is negligible, and to establish this fact we first prove an auxiliary result.

Lemma 13.1
.

For some
\eta>0
, the estimate

(13.1)

\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta N}),\quad N\to\infty,

holds true uniformly for
s
and
t
as in Assumptions
2.7
.

Proof.

Recall that
\sigma
was given in (
10.3
). For the proof of (
13.1
), we recall that the definition of
\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
implies that
(x-\mathsf{a})\geq\epsilon
, where
\epsilon>0
is such that
\mathsf{a}+\epsilon
is the point of intersection of
U_{\mathsf{a}}
with
(\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b})
. Likewise, the very definition of
\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
says that this set has at most
N(\mathsf{b}-\mathsf{a})
points. This way

\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)}=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}}\frac{1}{1+\ee^{tN(x-\mathsf{a})+s}}\leq N(\mathsf{b}-\mathsf{a})\ee^{-t\epsilon N-s}.

For us, we always have
s=\mathcal{O}(N^{1/2})
, and the inequality above implies (
13.1
) immediately.
∎

To estimate the last term on the right-hand side of (
12.37
), observe first that
\phi_{+}^{\prime}
is continuous on
(\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b})\supset\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
and independent of
N
, and therefore this function is bounded for
x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
, uniformly in
N
. Although
\mathsf{E}
does depend on
N
, Assumptions
2.4
ensures
\mathsf{E}
remains bounded uniformly as
N\to\infty
. Finally, an exact calculation shows that

\left|\frac{\sigma^{\prime}(x{\,|\,}s)}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)}\right|=\frac{Nt|x-\mathsf{a}|}{1+\ee^{tN(x-\mathsf{a})+s}}\leq Nt|x-\mathsf{a}|.

Thus,

2N\phi_{+}^{\prime}(x)-\mathsf{E}^{\prime}(x)-\frac{\sigma^{\prime}(x{\,|\,}s)}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)}=\mathcal{O}(N),\quad N\to\infty,

uniformly for
x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
and uniformly in
s
, and combining this estimate with (
13.1
), we update (
12.37
) to

(13.2)

\log\frac{\mathsf{Z}_{N}^{\sigma}(S)}{\mathsf{Z}^{\sigma}_{N}(s)}=\mathsf{S}(S,s)-\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{a}}}\log\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}S)}+\mathsf{R}(S,s)+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta N}),\quad N\to\infty,

where the error term is uniform for
S,s
as in Assumptions
2.7
. Next, we carry out the asymptotic analysis of
\mathsf{R}
.

13.2.
Analysis of
\mathsf{R}

Recall that
\mathsf{R}
was defined in (
12.31
), and we start estimating the terms
\mathsf{J}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
therein. These terms involve sums of values of
x
in each of the intervals

(0,\mathsf{a}-\epsilon]\supset\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})},\quad[\mathsf{a}+\epsilon,\mathsf{b}-\epsilon]\supset\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})},\quad[\mathsf{b}+\epsilon,\infty)\supset\mathcal{X}_{3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}.

where we recall that
\mathcal{X}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
,
\mathcal{X}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
were introduced in (
12.16
).

On
(0,\mathsf{a}-\epsilon]\cup[\mathsf{a}+\epsilon,\mathsf{b}-\epsilon]\cup[\mathsf{b}+\epsilon,\infty)
, we use (
11.34
) and obtain

\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{S}_{+}(x)=\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{G}_{+}(x)+\mathbf{G}_{+}(x)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{R}_{+}(x)\mathbf{G}_{+}(x).

Using the expansion (
11.18
) and the boundedness of
\mathbf{R}
, for
x\in(0,\mathsf{a}-\delta)\cup(\mathsf{a}+\delta,\mathsf{b}-\delta)\cup(\mathsf{b}+\delta,\infty)
we obtain

(13.3)

\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{S}_{+}(x)=x^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\left(\frac{1}{2x}\bm{\sigma}_{3}+\mathcal{O}(1)\right)x^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2},

where the error term above is uniform for both
N\to\infty
and also uniform for
x
in the refereed intervals. Recall that
\mathsf{J}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
are defined by the integrals in (
12.13
), with the sums over
\mathcal{X}_{k}
being replaced by sums over
\mathcal{X}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
. Using that
\mathsf{E}
is bounded on compacts, and that
\phi_{+}
is purely imaginary on
(\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b})\supset\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
(see Proposition
11.4
), from (
13.3
) we obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{J}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}(S,s)=\frac{1}{N}\int_{s}^{S}\mathcal{O}(1)\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}}\frac{\ee^{2N\phi_{+}(x)}(\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1)}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}\mathrm{d}v,

\displaystyle\mathsf{J}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}(S,s)=-\frac{1}{N}\int_{s}^{S}\mathcal{O}(1)\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}}(\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1)\mathrm{d}v,

\displaystyle\mathsf{J}_{3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}(S,s)=-\frac{1}{N}\int_{s}^{S}\mathcal{O}(1)\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}}\ee^{-2N\phi_{+}(x)+\mathsf{E}(x)}(\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1)\mathrm{d}v,

where the error term is uniform in all the parameters. Along
\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
, the function
2\re\phi_{+}
is strictly negative (see Proposition
11.4
and Proposition
11.2
–(d)), say
2\re\phi_{+}(x)\leq-\eta
. On the other hand, using the basic inequality
\ee^{u}/(1+\ee^{u})^{2}\leq 1
, valid for every real value
u
, we see that the factor involving
\sigma
in
\mathsf{J}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
above is bounded by
1
. Thus, we just concluded that

\mathsf{J}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}(S,s)=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-N\eta}),\quad N\to\infty,

uniformly for
S,s
within any of the regimes from Assumptions
2.7
. Next, for
\mathsf{J}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
, we use the explicit expression for
\sigma
to see that

0\leq\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1\leq\ee^{-tN\delta-s}
for every
x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}\subset(\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b})
, and therefore once again

\mathsf{J}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}(S,s)=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-N\eta}),\quad N\to\infty,

again uniformly for
S,s
. Finally, for
\mathsf{J}_{3}
, we use that
\phi(x)\sim\mathsf{V}(x)
as
x\to\infty
, and Assumptions
2.4
to ensure that
\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}}\ee^{-2N\phi(x)+\mathsf{E}(x)}
is bounded, and then use again the explicit expression for
\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1
and obtain once more that

\mathsf{J}_{3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}(S,s)=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-N\eta}),\quad N\to\infty,

uniformly for
S,s
within the regimes from Assumptions
2.7
. Recall that
\mathsf{R}
was defined in (
12.31
). This estimate concludes the analysis of the terms in
\mathsf{R}
involving the outer sets
\mathcal{X}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
, and we now move to the terms involving the inner sets.

To cope with
\mathsf{J}_{3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
we unwrap
\mathbf{S}\mapsto\mathbf{R}
on
(\mathsf{b}-\epsilon,\mathsf{b}+\epsilon)\supset\mathcal{X}_{3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
. Therein, we use (
11.34
) and (
11.32
) and obtain

\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{S}_{+}(x)=-\left(\frac{1}{2}\frac{\sigma^{\prime}(x)}{\sigma(x)}+\frac{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}(x)}{2}+N\phi_{+}(x)\right)\bm{\sigma}_{3}+\sigma(x)^{\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\ee^{(\mathsf{E}(x)/2+N\phi_{+}(x))\bm{\sigma}_{3}}\\
\times\left[\mathbf{\Delta}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}(x)+\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}(x)^{-1}\left((\mathbf{\Delta}_{z}\widetilde{\mathbf{Ai}})(\xi)+\widetilde{\mathbf{Ai}}(\xi)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{R}(x)\widetilde{\mathbf{Ai}}(\xi)\right)\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}(x)\right]\sigma(x)^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/2}\ee^{-(\mathsf{E}(x)/2+N\phi_{+}(x))\bm{\sigma}_{3}},

where
\xi=N\varphi(z)
is as in (
11.32
), and we wrote
\mathbf{\Delta}=\mathbf{\Delta}_{z}
to emphasize that it involves a derivative with respect to
z=x
instead of with respect to
\xi
. Therefore

\mathsf{J}_{3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\ee^{-2N\phi_{+}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)}\\
\times\left[\mathbf{\Delta}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}(x)+\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}(x)^{-1}\left((\mathbf{\Delta}_{z}\widetilde{\mathbf{Ai}})(\xi)+\widetilde{\mathbf{Ai}}(\xi)^{-1}\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{R}(x)\widetilde{\mathbf{Ai}}(\xi)\right)\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}(x)\right]_{21,+}\mathrm{d}v.

Using that
\sigma
as well as its derivatives remain bounded as
N\to\infty
uniformly for
z
near
\mathsf{b}
, one can see that both
\widetilde{(}\mathbf{A})^{\pm 1}
and
\widetilde{(}\mathbf{A})
grows at most polynomially in
N
as
N\to\infty
. The same considerations also hold for the terms involving
\widetilde{{\mathbf{Ai}}}
above. All in all, we see that the
(2,1)
-entry inside the integral grows at most polynomially in
N
. On the other hand, exploring the decay of the remaining terms in a similar way as did for
\mathcal{X}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
previously, we obtain

\mathsf{J}_{3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta N}),\quad N\to\infty,

again uniformly in
S,s
. Recall that
\mathsf{R}
is defined in (
12.31
). This last estimate with the ones obtained previously for
\mathsf{J}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
show that

(13.4)

\mathsf{R}(S,s)=\mathsf{K}_{1,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{1,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{2,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta N}),\quad N\to\infty.

We now estimate the remaining terms, which we recall were introduced in (
12.21
) and (
12.25
).

Thanks to the relation (
11.28
), Proposition
11.17
and Theorem
11.21
, the expressions for
\mathsf{K}_{j,k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
’s in (
12.21
) and (
12.25
) may be updated to

(13.5)

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{1,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}=\frac{1}{N^{2/3}}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}\left[\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathcal{O}(1)\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\right]_{21}\mathrm{d}v,

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{1,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}=\frac{1}{N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}\left[\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathcal{O}(1)\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\right]_{21}\mathrm{d}v,

and

(13.6)

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}=\frac{1}{N^{2/3}}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}(\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1)\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathcal{O}(1)\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\right]_{12}\mathrm{d}v,

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{2,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}=\frac{1}{N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}(\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1)\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathcal{O}(1)\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\right]_{12}\mathrm{d}v.

These expressions now involve only
\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau}
, and with the help of the estimates already provided for this term we now indicate why these terms are in fact decaying; such analysis will depend on whether we are in the subcritical, critical or supercritical regimes of
S,s
.

For the calculations in the coming subsections, we recall that the change of variables

x\mapsto\zeta=\tau^{2}\varphi(x),\quad\tau=N^{1/3},

was defined in (
11.23
), and it is such that

(13.7)

\zeta({z_{N}(s)})=N^{2/3}\varphi({z_{N}(s)})={\zeta_{N}(s)},\quad\text{and}\quad\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)-1=\ee^{-tN(x-{z_{N}(s)})}=\ee^{N^{1/3}\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta{\,|\,}s)},

see (
11.25
). Recall that
\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
was defined in (
12.35
), and introduce

(13.8)

x^{-}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\min\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})},\quad x^{+}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\max\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})},\quad\kappa\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=-\varphi(x^{\pm})>0.

With an adjustment of
x^{\pm}
if necessary, we may without lost of generality assume that
\varphi(x^{-})=\kappa=-\varphi(x^{+})
. The conformal map sends points in
\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
to
[{\zeta_{N}(s)},\kappa N^{2/3}]
, and points in
\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
to
[-\kappa N^{2/3},{\zeta_{N}(s)}]
. We use the identifications above extensively in what comes next.

13.2.1.
Rough estimates on
\mathsf{R}
in the subcritical case

We start with the estimates in the subcritical case, in which
{\zeta_{N}(s)}<\mathsf{a}
. In this case, in particular,
0<\zeta(x)<{\zeta_{N}(s)}
if, and only if,
x\in(z_{N}(s),\mathsf{a})\subset\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
.

With an application of Proposition
9.1
–(ii) to (
13.5
) we are ensured of the estimate

(13.9)

\mathsf{K}_{1,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}+\mathsf{K}_{1,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}=\mathcal{O}(N^{-2/3})\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}}\;\mathrm{d}v.

Having in mind that
0<\zeta(x)<{\zeta_{N}(s)}
if, and only if,
x\in({z_{N}(s)},\mathsf{a})\subset\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
, we use Proposition
9.1
–(ii) again, now in (
13.6
), and write in a similar way

\mathsf{K}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}+\mathsf{K}_{2,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}=\mathcal{O}(N^{-2/3})\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)\cap(z_{N}(v),\mathsf{a})\end{subarray}}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\;\mathrm{d}v\\
+\mathcal{O}(N^{-2/3})\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)\cap[\mathsf{a},\mathsf{a}+\epsilon)\end{subarray}}(\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1)(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\;\mathrm{d}v

To obtain this identity, we emphasize that we used the algebraic identity (
6.28
) with the choice
\beta=\sigma=1+\ee^{N^{1/3}\mathsf{P}_{N}}
for the estimate on
\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)\cap(z_{N}(s),\mathsf{a})
, and we used that
\zeta(x)_{-}^{3/2}\in\mathrm{i}\mathbb{R}
and that
1\leq\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)\leq 2
for
x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)\setminus(z_{N}(s),\mathsf{a})
.

Observe that
\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta)=-|\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta)|
for
\zeta<{\zeta_{N}(s)}
, that is, for
x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
, see Proposition
6.3
. Also, using the simple inequality
\ee^{-u}/(1+\ee^{-u})^{2}\leq\ee^{-|u|}
for
u\in\mathbb{R}
, we bound
(\sigma-1)/\sigma^{2}\leq\ee^{-N^{1/3}|\mathsf{P}_{N}|}=\ee^{-N^{1/3}\mathsf{P}_{N}}
for
\zeta>{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}
, that is, for
x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
. Hence, the latter two estimates on
\mathsf{K}_{j,k}
can be combined to

(13.10)

\mathsf{K}_{1,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}+\mathsf{K}_{1,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}+\mathsf{K}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}+\mathsf{K}_{2,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}=\mathcal{O}(N^{-2/3})\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\ee^{-N^{1/3}|\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta)|-\frac{4}{3}\re\zeta^{3/2}_{-}}\,\mathrm{d}v,

recalling again that
\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
is as in (
12.35
).

To estimate these sums, let us introduce

(13.11)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{X}}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}={\mathcal{X}}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}{\,|\,}\zeta(x)<\frac{{\zeta_{N}(s)}}{3}\right\},

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{X}}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}={\mathcal{X}}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}{\,|\,}\frac{{\zeta_{N}(s)}}{3}\leq\zeta(x)\leq 3{\zeta_{N}(s)}\right\},

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{X}}_{3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}={\mathcal{X}}_{3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}{\,|\,}\zeta(x)>3{\zeta_{N}(s)}\right\}.

Clearly these sets are pairwise distinct and

\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}={\mathcal{X}}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}\cup{\mathcal{X}}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}\cup{\mathcal{X}}_{3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}.

We estimate the sum in (
13.10
) by estimating it in each of the sets
\mathcal{X}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
.

We start bounding the sum over
\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
. Over there, we simply bound
\re\zeta_{-}^{3/2}\geq 0
to simplify

(13.12)

\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(v)}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\ee^{-N^{1/3}|\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta)|-\frac{4}{3}\re\zeta^{3/2}_{-}}\leq\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(v)}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\ee^{-N^{1/3}|\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta)|}.

Points in
\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(v)
may be parametrized as
\{x_{k}\}_{k\geq 1}
with

x_{k}\leq\frac{z_{N}(v)}{2}-\frac{k}{N},\quad\text{and therefore}\quad N^{1/3}|\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta(x_{k}))|=Nt|x_{k}-z_{N}(v)|\geq tN\frac{z_{N}(v)}{2}+tk.

Also, clearly
|\zeta|^{1/2}=N^{1/3}|\varphi(x)|\leq CN^{1/3}
, for some constant
C>0
independent of
v,N
, simply because
\varphi
is conformal. We update (
13.12
) to

\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(v)}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\ee^{-N^{1/3}|\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta)|-\frac{4}{3}\re\zeta^{3/2}_{-}}\leq CN^{1/3}\ee^{-\eta Nz_{N}(v)}\sum_{k\geq 1}\ee^{-tk}\leq C^{\prime}N^{1/3}\ee^{-\eta Nz_{N}(v)}

for some positive constants
C,C^{\prime},\eta
, all independent of
N,v
.

Similar arguments also apply to the sum over
\mathcal{X}_{3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
, and we conclude that for some
\eta>0
,

(13.13)

\frac{1}{N^{2/3}}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(v)\cup\mathcal{X}_{3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(v)}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\ee^{-N^{1/3}|\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta)|-\frac{4}{3}\re\zeta^{3/2}_{-}}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{N^{1/3}}\ee^{-\eta Nz_{N}(v)}\right),

uniformly for
v
in the subcritical regime.

Next, we estimate the sum over
\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
. Therein, we always have
\re\zeta^{3/2}_{-}=\zeta^{3/2}>0
, and we use (
6.17
) to bound
\ee^{-|N^{1/3}\mathsf{P}_{N}|}\leq C\ee^{-\eta N^{1/3}|\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)|}
, for some constants
\eta,C>0
. All in all, and using in addition that
|\zeta|\geq\frac{1}{3}\zeta_{N}(v)
for
x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
, we obtain

(13.14)

\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(v)}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\ee^{-N^{1/3}|\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta)|-\frac{4}{3}\re\zeta^{3/2}_{-}}\leq C\zeta_{N}(v)^{1/2}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(v)}\ee^{-2\eta N^{1/3}|\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)|-\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}}

for yet new constants
C,\eta>0
; the constant
2
in front of
\eta
is not an accident, and its presence is explored in what follows. We now seek for the minimum of
\zeta\mapsto\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}+\eta N^{1/3}|\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)|
for
x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(v)
. A direct calculation shows that its critical points are solutions to
\frac{2}{3}\zeta^{1/2}=\pm\eta N^{1/3}
, where the sign
\pm
depends on whether
\zeta>\zeta_{N}(v)
or
\zeta<\zeta_{N}(v)
. Such critical points are
\mathcal{O}(N^{1/3})
, and because we are in the subcritical regime none of them belongs to the interval
[\zeta_{N}(v)/3,3\zeta_{N}(v)]\supset\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
. Hence,

\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}+\eta N^{1/3}|\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)|\geq\min_{\xi=\frac{\zeta_{N}(v)}{3},\zeta_{N}(v),3\zeta_{N}(v)}\left(\frac{4}{3}\xi^{3/2}+\eta N^{1/3}|\xi-\zeta_{N}(v)|\right),\quad\text{for every }\zeta\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}.

Once again because we are in the subcritical regime, we have that
N^{1/3}\zeta_{N}(v)\gg\zeta_{N}(v)^{3/2}
, and the minimum above is attained precisely at
\xi=\zeta_{N}(v)
. Returning this information into (
13.14
), we further estimate

(13.15)

\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(v)}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\ee^{-N^{1/3}|\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta)|-\frac{4}{3}\re\zeta^{3/2}_{-}}\leq C\zeta_{N}(v)^{1/2}\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta_{N}(v)^{3/2}}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(v)}\ee^{-\eta N^{1/3}|\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)|}.

Writing
\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)=N^{2/3}(\varphi(x)-\varphi(z_{N}(v)))
and using that
\varphi
is a conformal map independent of
N
, we in fact may bound
|\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)|\geq cN^{2/3}|x-z_{N}(v)|
. Using then that the points
x
’s belong to
\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we obtain that the remaining sum on the right-hand side above is finite. Hence, we just concluded that

(13.16)

\frac{1}{N^{2/3}}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(v)}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\ee^{-N^{1/3}|\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta)|-\frac{4}{3}\re\zeta^{3/2}_{-}}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\zeta_{N}(v)^{1/2}}{N^{2/3}}\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta_{N}(v)^{3/2}}\right).

We now plug (
13.13
) and (
13.16
) into (
13.10
). Using once again that
Nz_{N}(v)=N^{1/3}\zeta_{N}(v)\gg\zeta_{N}(v)^{3/2}
for
v
in the subcritical regime, we concluded the next result.

Proposition 13.2
.

The estimate

\mathsf{K}_{1,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{1,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{2,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)=\mathcal{O}(N^{-2/3})\int_{s}^{S}\zeta_{N}(v)^{1/2}\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta_{N}(v)^{3/2}}\mathrm{d}v,\quad N\to\infty,

is valid uniformly for
s\leq S
and both within the subcritical regime.
is valid uniformly for
s\leq S
and both within the subcritical regime.

13.2.2.
Rough estimates on
\mathsf{R}
in the critical case

As before, we first summarize the contributions of
\mathsf{K}_{j,k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
into simpler estimates.

Recall that
\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)
and
\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)
were introduced in (
12.16
). Using Proposition
9.1
–(i) in the representation (
13.5
), we obtain

(13.17)

\mathsf{K}_{1,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{1,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)=\mathcal{O}(N^{-2/3})\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta_{-}^{3/2}-2\zeta_{\tau}(v)\zeta_{-}^{1/2}}\mathrm{d}v,

where we used that
\zeta=\zeta(x)\geq\zeta_{N}(v)
for
x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)
.

Next, using (
6.28
) and again Proposition
9.1
–(i), for
\zeta_{N}(v)-r<\zeta<\zeta_{N}(v)
we estimate

(13.18)

\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathcal{O}(1)\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\right]_{12}=\mathcal{O}(1)\frac{(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta_{-}^{3/2}-2\zeta_{N}(v)\zeta_{-}^{1/2}}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}.

The exponent
\frac{2}{3}\zeta_{-}^{3/2}+\zeta_{N}(v)\zeta_{-}^{1/2}
is purely imaginary on the negative axis, and it is bounded on bounded sets of the positive real axis. Hence, because we are assuming
\zeta<\zeta_{N}(v)
and the latter is bounded, in this last estimate the exponential terms are in fact bounded, and we just concluded that

(13.19)

\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathcal{O}(1)\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\right]_{12}=\mathcal{O}(1)\frac{(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}},

valid for
\zeta_{N}(v)-r<\zeta<\zeta_{N}(v)
.

We now extend this estimate to
\zeta<\zeta_{N}(v)-r
as well. First we write, again using (
6.28
)

(13.20)

\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathcal{O}(1)\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\right]_{12}\\
=-\frac{1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathcal{O}(1)\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)\mathbf{E}_{21}\right)\right]_{21}.

Thanks to Proposition
6.9
–(ii), the factor
\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)=1+\ee^{\tau\mathsf{P}_{\tau}(\zeta)}
is bounded for
\zeta=\zeta(x)\in(-\infty,\zeta_{N}(v))=\Gamma_{4}^{\zeta_{N}(v)}
. Plugging the asymptotic formula from Proposition
9.1
–(i) into (
13.20
), we conclude that

\left[\left(\mathbf{I}+\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)^{-1}\mathcal{O}(1)\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\mathbf{E}_{12}}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)}\right)\right]_{12}\\
=\frac{1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}\left[\mathcal{O}(1)\ee^{(\frac{2}{3}\zeta_{-}^{3/2}+\zeta_{N}(v)\zeta_{-}^{1/2})\bm{\sigma}_{3}}\mathcal{O}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\ee^{-(\frac{2}{3}\zeta_{-}^{3/2}+\zeta_{N}(v)\zeta_{-}^{1/2})\bm{\sigma}_{3}}\mathcal{O}(1)\right]_{21},

valid for
\zeta<\zeta_{N}(v)-r
. Proceeding as we did to obtain (
13.19
) from (
13.18
), we see that the exponential terms above are bounded, in turn extending (
13.19
) to the whole interval
\zeta<\zeta_{N}(v)
. Plugging the result asymptotic formula into (
13.6
), we obtain the estimate

\mathsf{K}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{2,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)=\mathcal{O}(N^{-2/3})\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\mathrm{d}v.

We combine this estimate with (
13.17
), summarizing them as

\mathsf{K}_{1,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{1,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{2,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)=\mathcal{O}(N^{-2/3})\\
\times\int_{s}^{S}\left[\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta_{-}^{3/2}-2\zeta_{N}(v)\zeta_{-}^{1/2}}+\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\right]\mathrm{d}v.

Using the simple inequality
\ee^{-u}/(1+\ee^{-u})^{2}\leq\ee^{-|u|}
for
u\in\mathbb{R}
, we bound
(\sigma-1)/\sigma^{2}\leq\ee^{-N^{1/3}|\mathsf{P}_{N}|}
for
x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)
, which corresponds to
\zeta<\zeta_{N}(v)
. Recalling (
6.17
), we obtain

(13.21)

\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\leq\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\ee^{-\eta N^{1/3}|\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)|}.

Writing
\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)=N^{2/3}(\varphi(x)-\varphi(z_{N}(v)))
and using that
\varphi
is a conformal map independent of
N
, we in fact may bound
|\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)|\geq cN^{2/3}|x-z_{N}(v)|
. Since
\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)\subset\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we obtain that the remaining sum on the right-hand side above is finite, and in fact

(13.22)

\frac{1}{N^{2/3}}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{|\zeta_{N}(v)|^{1/2}}{N^{2/3}}\right).

Next, using again that
(\sigma-1)/\sigma^{2}\leq\ee^{-N^{1/3}|\mathsf{P}_{N}|}
for
\zeta<{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}
(that is, for
x\in\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)
) and the estimate (
6.17
) once again, we bound

(13.23)

\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta_{-}^{3/2}-2\zeta_{N}(v)\zeta_{-}^{1/2}}\leq\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\ee^{-\eta N^{1/3}|\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)|-\frac{4}{3}\zeta_{-}^{3/2}-2\zeta_{N}(v)\zeta_{-}^{1/2}}.

We stress once again that
\zeta>\zeta_{N}(v)
for
x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)
. If
\zeta_{N}(v)>0
, then
\frac{4}{3}\zeta_{-}^{3/2}+2\zeta_{N}(v)\zeta_{-}^{1/2}>0
for every
x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)
. For
\zeta_{N}(v)<0
, we have
\frac{4}{3}\zeta_{-}^{3/2}+2\zeta_{N}(v)\zeta_{-}^{1/2}\in\mathrm{i}\mathbb{R}
for
\zeta_{N}(v)\leq\zeta<0
. Furthermore, still for
\zeta_{N}(v)<0
, the function
[0,+\infty)\ni\zeta\mapsto\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}+2\zeta_{N}(v)\zeta^{1/2}
has a unique local minimum at the point
\zeta_{N}(v)/2
, where it attains the value
-2^{3/2}|\zeta_{N}(v)|^{3/2}/3
. Since we are in the critical regime, we obtain in particular that
\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}+2\zeta_{N}(v)\zeta^{1/2}
remains bounded from below for
\zeta>0
. With these information in mind, we update (
13.23
) to the estimate

(13.24)

\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)-1}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}v)^{2}}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta_{-}^{3/2}-2\zeta_{N}(v)\zeta_{-}^{1/2}}\leq C\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)}(1+|\zeta|^{1/2})\ee^{-\eta N^{1/3}|\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)|}.

Proceeding as we did for (
13.22
), we see that the remaining sum above is
\mathcal{O}(|\zeta_{N}(v)|^{1/2})
.

In summary, we concluded

Proposition 13.3
.

The estimate

\mathsf{K}_{1,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{1,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{2,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)=\mathcal{O}(N^{-2/3})\int^{S}_{s}|\zeta_{N}(v)|^{1/2}\mathrm{d}v,\quad N\to\infty,

is valid uniformly for
s\leq S
and both within the critical regime.

13.2.3.
Rough estimates on
\mathsf{R}
in the supercritical case

In addition to denoting
\zeta=\zeta(x)
, in what follows it is also convenient to denote

\xi=\xi(x)=\xi(x{\,|\,}s)={\zeta_{N}(s)}^{2}(\zeta(x)-{\zeta_{N}(s)}).

Finally, we now rewrite the error terms
\mathsf{K}_{j,k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
in the supercritical case. For that, we will use once again the identities (
13.5
)–(
13.6
), combined with the asymptotics from Proposition
9.1
. In particular, Proposition
9.1
–(iii) provides asymptotics for
\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}
which are different depending on certain neighborhoods of
{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}=\zeta({z_{N}(s)})
. Based on these neighborhoods we introduce the pairwise disjoint sets

(13.25)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{X}}_{k,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}={\mathcal{X}}_{k,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}{\,|\,}|\zeta(x)-{\zeta_{N}(s)}|\leq\frac{\delta}{{\zeta_{N}(s)}^{2}}\right\}=\left\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}{\,|\,}|\xi(x)|\leq\delta\right\},

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{X}}_{k,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}={\mathcal{X}}_{k,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}{\,|\,}\frac{\delta}{{\zeta_{N}(s)}^{2}}<|\zeta(x)-{\zeta_{N}(s)}|\leq\delta\right\}=\left\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}{\,|\,}\delta<|\xi(x)|\leq\delta{\zeta_{N}(s)}^{2}\right\},

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{X}}_{k,3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}={\mathcal{X}}_{k,3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}{\,|\,}|\zeta(x)-{\zeta_{N}(s)}|>\delta\right\}=\left\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}{\,|\,}|\xi(x)|>\delta{\zeta_{N}(s)}^{2}\right\}.

The value
\delta>0
may be chosen arbitrarily small but fixed.

Clearly

\mathcal{X}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}={\mathcal{X}}_{k,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s)\cup{\mathcal{X}}_{k,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s)\cup{\mathcal{X}}_{k,3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s),\quad\text{for every }s.

Furthermore,
\xi\geq 0
for
x\in\mathcal{X}_{1,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
, whereas
\xi\leq 0
for
x\in\mathcal{X}_{2,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
,
j=1,2,3
.

We simplify the expressions in Proposition
9.1
–(iii) to

\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)=\\
\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}(|{\zeta_{N}(s)}|^{5/4})\mathbf{B}_{-}(\xi)(\mathbf{I}+\mathsf{B}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon},-}(\xi)\mathbf{E}_{12}),&x\in\mathcal{X}_{1,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s),\\
\mathcal{O}(|{\zeta_{N}(s)}|^{5/4})\mathbf{B}_{-}(\xi)(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{E}_{21})(\mathbf{I}+\mathsf{B}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon},-}(\xi)\mathbf{E}_{12})(\mathbf{I}-(1+\ee^{N^{1/3}\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta)})\mathbf{E}_{21}),&x\in\mathcal{X}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s),\\
\mathcal{O}(|{\zeta_{N}(s)}|^{5/4})\mathbf{B}_{-}(\xi),&x\in\mathcal{X}_{1,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s)\cup\mathcal{X}_{2,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s),\\
\mathcal{O}({\zeta_{N}(s)}(\zeta-{\zeta_{N}(s)})^{1/4})\ee^{-\left(\frac{2}{3}(\zeta-{\zeta_{N}(s)})_{-}^{3/2}+{\zeta_{N}(s)}(\zeta-{\zeta_{N}(s)})^{1/2}_{-}\right)\bm{\sigma}_{3}},&x\in\mathcal{X}_{1,3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s)\cup\mathcal{X}_{2,3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s).\end{cases}

In obtaining such expressions, we used the explicit expression (
7.68
) for
\mathbf{L}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}
, as well as the relation (
7.53
) between
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\tau}
and
\mathsf{P}_{\tau}=\mathsf{P}_{N}
. We also recall that
\mathbf{B}
and
\mathsf{B}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon}}
are given in (
7.61
) and (
7.66
), respectively.

When
|\xi|
is large, the behavior of
\mathbf{B}
is governed by (
7.59
), when
|\xi|
gets close to
0
the behavior of
\mathbf{B}
is given in (
7.60
), and for
|\xi|
in compacts of
(0,+\infty)
the matrix
\mathbf{B}
is bounded. With these observations in mind, we update the above estimates to

\mathbf{\Phi}_{\tau,-}(\zeta)=\\
\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}(|{\zeta_{N}(s)}|^{5/4})(\mathbf{I}+\left(\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\log\xi+\mathsf{B}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon},-}(\xi)\right)\mathbf{E}_{12}),&x\in\mathcal{X}_{1,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s),\\
\mathcal{O}(|{\zeta_{N}(s)}|^{5/4})(\mathbf{I}+\left(\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\log\xi+\mathsf{B}_{\mathbf{\Upsilon},-}(\xi)\right)\mathbf{E}_{12})(\mathbf{I}-(1+\ee^{N^{1/3}\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta)})\mathbf{E}_{21}),&x\in\mathcal{X}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s),\\
\mathcal{O}(|{\zeta_{N}(s)}|^{5/4}(1+|\xi|^{1/4}))\ee^{\xi^{1/2}_{-}\bm{\sigma}_{3}},&x\in\mathcal{X}_{1,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s)\cup\mathcal{X}_{2,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s),\\
\mathcal{O}({\zeta_{N}(s)}(\zeta-{\zeta_{N}(s)})^{1/4})\ee^{-\left(\frac{2}{3}(\zeta-{\zeta_{N}(s)})_{-}^{3/2}+{\zeta_{N}(s)}(\zeta-{\zeta_{N}(s)})^{1/2}_{-}\right)\bm{\sigma}_{3}},&x\in\mathcal{X}_{1,3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s)\cup\mathcal{X}_{2,3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s).\end{cases}

We now use these asymptotic formulas to estimate the factors
\mathsf{K}_{k,j}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
. First off, using these formulas along
\mathcal{X}_{1,k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
and the already familiar estimate
\ee^{u}/(1+\ee^{u})^{2}\leq\ee^{-|u|}
, we update (
13.5
) to

(13.26)

\mathsf{K}_{1,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{1,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)=\mathcal{O}(N^{-2/3})\int_{s}^{S}\left(\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)\cup\mathcal{X}_{1,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)}|\zeta_{N}(v)|^{5/4}(1+|\xi|^{1/4})\ee^{-Nt|x-z_{N}(v)|+2\xi^{1/2}}\right.\\
\left.+\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1,3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)}|\zeta_{N}(v)|(\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v))^{1/4}\ee^{-Nt|x-z_{N}(v)|-\frac{4}{3}(\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v))^{3/2}-2\zeta_{N}(v)(\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v))^{1/2}}\right)\mathrm{d}v.

In the above and in what follows, we emphasize that
\xi=\xi(x{\,|\,}v)
depends both in
x
and also on the variable of integration
v
, but we omit this dependence.

Likewise, we now apply the asymptotic formulas to (
13.6
). In addition to plugging them in, we also use that
(\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v))_{-}^{1/2},(\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v))_{-}^{3/2}\in\mathrm{i}\mathbb{R}
for
x\in\mathcal{X}_{2,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)
,
\xi_{-}^{1/2}\in\mathrm{i}\mathbb{R}
for
x\in\mathcal{X}_{2,3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)
, and also identity (
6.28
) to simplify the expression on
\mathcal{X}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)
. The resulting estimate is

(13.27)

\mathsf{K}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{2,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)=\mathcal{O}(N^{-2/3})\int_{s}^{S}\left(\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)\cup\mathcal{X}_{2,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)}|\zeta_{N}(v)|^{5/4}(1+|\xi|^{1/4})\ee^{-Nt|x-z_{N}(v)|}\right.\\
\left.+\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2,3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)}|\zeta_{N}(v)||\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)|^{1/4}\ee^{-Nt|x-z_{N}(v)|}\right)\mathrm{d}v.

We now bound each of the sums appearing in (
13.26
)–(
13.27
).

For
x\in\mathcal{X}_{1,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)\cup\mathcal{X}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)
, notice that
|\xi(x)|\leq\delta
for some positive universal constant
c>0
, and therefore

(13.28)

\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)}|\zeta_{N}(v)|^{5/4}(1+|\xi|^{1/4})\ee^{-Nt|x-z_{N}(v)|+2\xi^{1/2}}+\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)}|\zeta_{N}(v)|^{5/4}(1+|\xi|^{1/4})\ee^{-Nt|x-z_{N}(v)|}\\
\leq\tilde{C}|\zeta_{N}(v)|^{5/4}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)\cup\mathcal{X}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)}\ee^{-Nt|x-z_{N}(v)|}\leq C|\zeta_{N}(v)|^{5/4}

for some new constants
C,\tilde{C}>0
, which are uniform for
s
within the supercritical regime. In the last step, we simply used that the remaining series is finite because
\mathcal{X}_{j,k}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)\subset\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.

Next, we analyze the sums over
\chi_{k,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)
. For that, we use the explicit form (
11.23
) of the conformal map
x\mapsto\zeta
to bound
Nt|x-z_{N}(v)|\geq 2cN^{1/3}|\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)|
for some constant
c>0
independent of
s
, so that

Nt|x-z_{N}(v)|-2\xi^{1/2}\geq\frac{t}{2}N|x-z_{N}(v)|+N^{1/3}c|\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)|-2\xi^{1/2}=\frac{t}{2}N|x-z_{N}(v)|+\frac{cN^{1/3}}{\zeta_{N}(v)^{2}}\xi-2\xi^{1/2}.

The map
(0,\infty)\ni\xi\mapsto cN^{1/3}\xi^{2}/\zeta_{N}(v)^{2}\xi-2\xi^{1/2}
has a unique critical point at
(2\zeta_{N}(v)/cN^{1/3})^{2}
. Since we are in the supercritical regime, when
\zeta_{N}(s)=\mathcal{O}(s/N^{1/3})
, we can choose
s_{0}>0
sufficiently large in such a way that this critical point is smaller than
\delta/2
. Consequently, the function
\xi\mapsto cN^{1/3}\xi/\zeta_{N}(v)^{2}-2\xi^{1/2}
is increasing on
(\delta,+\infty)\supset\mathcal{X}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)
, attaining its smallest value precisely at
\xi=\delta
, where its value is bounded bounded from below by a constant
c>0
independent of
N
. We just concluded that

(13.29)

Nt|x-z_{N}(v)|-2\xi^{1/2}\geq\frac{t}{2}N|x-z_{N}(v)|+c

for every
x\in\mathcal{X}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)
, and therefore

(13.30)

\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)}|\zeta_{N}(v)|^{5/4}(1+|\xi|^{1/4})\ee^{-Nt|x-z_{N}(v)|+2\xi^{1/2}}+\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)}|\zeta_{N}(v)|^{5/4}(1+|\xi|^{1/4})\ee^{-Nt|x-z_{N}(v)|}\\
\leq\tilde{C}|\zeta_{N}(v)|^{5/4}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)\cup\mathcal{X}_{2,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)}(1+|\xi|^{1/4})\ee^{-\frac{t}{2}N|x-z_{N}(v)|}\leq C|\zeta_{N}(v)|^{5/4}

for some new constants
\tilde{C},C>0
, and where in the last inequality we used again the fact that the summand involves
x\in\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.

Finally, for
x\in\mathcal{X}_{1,3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)
we have
|\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)|\geq\delta
which implies
N|x-z_{N}(v)|\geq 4cN^{1/3}
for some new value
c>0
