. Using the same arguments we applied for
x\in\mathcal{X}_{1,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)
(see (
13.29
)), we bound

Nt|x-z_{N}(v)|+\frac{4}{3}(\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v))^{3/2}+2\zeta_{N}(v)(\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v))^{1/2}\\
\geq\frac{t}{4}N|x-z_{N}(v)|+cN^{1/3}+\frac{t}{2}N|x-z_{N}(v)|-2\xi^{1/2}\geq\frac{t}{4}N|x-z_{N}(v)|+cN^{1/3}+C,

valid for every
x\in\mathcal{X}_{1,3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)
. This way,

(13.31)

\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1,3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)}|\zeta_{N}(v)|(\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v))^{1/4}\ee^{-Nt|x-z_{N}(v)|-\frac{4}{3}(\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v))^{3/2}-2\zeta_{N}(v)(\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v))^{1/2}}\\
+\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{2,3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)}|\zeta_{N}(v)||\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)|^{1/4}\ee^{-Nt|x-z_{N}(v)|}\\
\leq C\ee^{-cN^{1/3}}|\zeta_{N}(v)|\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{1,3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)\cup\mathcal{X}_{2,3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)}|\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)|^{1/4}\ee^{-\frac{t}{4}N|x-z_{N}(v)|}\leq CN^{1/6}\ee^{-cN^{1/3}}|\zeta_{N}(v)|

and where in the last step we also used that
|\zeta|\leq\kappa N^{2/3}
for every
x\in\mathcal{X}_{1,3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)\cup\mathcal{X}_{2,3}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)
(see the comments after (
13.7
)), and the constants appearing in each step may be different.

To conclude the current section, using (
13.28
),(
13.30
), (
13.31
) in (
13.26
) and (
13.27
), we obtain

Proposition 13.4
.

The estimate

\mathsf{K}_{1,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{1,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{2,1}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)+\mathsf{K}_{2,2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(S,s)=\mathcal{O}(N^{-2/3})\int^{S}_{s}|\zeta_{N}(v)|^{5/4}\mathrm{d}v

is valid uniformly for
s\leq S
and both within the supercritical regime.

Observe that when writing this bound, we can simply ignore the exponentially small contributions coming from (
13.31
) because
\zeta_{N}(v)
grows algebraically in
N
.

13.2.4.
Conclusion of the estimates on
\mathsf{R}

For ease of reference for later, we now summarize the obtained estimates on
\mathsf{R}
.

Proposition 13.5
.

The error term
\mathsf{R}=\mathsf{R}(S,s)
in (
12.31
) satisfies the following estimates.

(i)

With

(13.32)

s_{N}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}}{t}\frac{s}{N^{1/3}},\quad S_{N}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}}{t}\frac{S}{N^{1/3}},

the estimate

\mathsf{R}(S,s)=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}s_{N}^{3/2}}-\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}S_{N}^{3/2}}}{N^{1/3}}\right)

is valid uniformly for
s\leq S
within the subcritical regime.

(ii)

With

\mathsf{R}(S,s)=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{|S|^{3/2}+|s|^{3/2}}{N^{5/6}}\right)

is valid uniformly for
s\leq S
within the critical regime.

(iii)

The estimate

\mathsf{R}(S,s)=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{|s|^{9/4}-|S|^{9/4}}{N^{13/12}}\right)

is valid uniformly for
s\leq S
within the supercritical regime.

Proof.

Recall that
\zeta_{N}=\mathcal{O}(s/N^{1/3})
(see (
6.6
)). For the proofs in any of the regimes, our starting point is (
13.4
).

For
s,S
in the subcritical regime, we use the expansion (
6.6
), the first identity in (
11.26
), and the change of variables
u=\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}v/(tN^{1/3})
, and as a result obtain

\int_{s}^{S}\zeta_{N}(v)^{1/2}\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta_{N}(v)^{4/3}}\mathrm{d}v=\frac{tN^{1/3}}{\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}}\left(1+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{N^{1/6}}\right)\right)\int_{s_{N}}^{S_{N}}u^{1/2}\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}u^{3/2}}\mathrm{d}u,\quad N\to\infty.

valid uniformly for
s,S
in the supercritical regime. The remaining integral can be computed exactly through the change of variables
\frac{4}{3}u^{3/2}=v
, and using (
13.4
) and Proposition
13.2
we conclude (i).

For
s,S
in the critical case, we estimate
|\zeta_{N}(v)|^{1/2}=\mathcal{O}(|v|^{1/2}/N^{1/6})
uniformly for
v\in[s,S]
. Integrating the estimate from Proposition
13.3
and using (
13.4
), part (ii) follows. With Proposition
13.4
in mind, part (iii) follows analogously.
∎

For
s,S
in the critical regime and
with the same sign
, the term
|S|^{3/2}+|s|^{3/2}
in Proposition
13.5
–(ii) could be replaced by the more refined term
||S|^{3/2}-|s|^{3/2}|
. But for our purposes, the estimate as stated is sufficient.

13.3.
Asymptotic analysis of
\mathsf{S}

Finally, we now analyze the quantity
\mathsf{S}
. Once again with the identifications (
13.7
)–(
13.8
) in mind, we rewrite the definition of
\mathsf{S}
from (
12.36
) as

(13.33)

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}(S,s)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}}\zeta^{\prime}(x)\mathcal{H}_{N}(\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(v){\,|\,}v)\frac{\ee^{-Nt(x-z_{N}(v))}}{(1+\ee^{-Nt(x-z_{N}(v))})^{2}}\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N^{1/3}}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{k\in N\mathcal{X}^{{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}}}\varphi^{\prime}\left(\frac{k}{N}\right)\mathcal{H}_{N}\left(\zeta\left(\frac{k}{N}\right)-\zeta_{N}(v)\Big|\,v\right)\frac{\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v}}{(1+\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v})^{2}}\;\mathrm{d}v.

Even though the argument
k/N
of the terms
\varphi^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{H}_{N}
indicate that the sum in (
13.33
) may be seen as a Riemann sum, the weight-like term
\ee^{(\cdot)}/(1+\ee^{(\cdot)})^{2}
already lives on a correct dominant scale, and the sum cannot be approximated by an integral. So our next goal is to estimate this sum directly.

The first ingredient is a key identity for the series obtained after formally setting
\varphi^{\prime}\mathcal{H}_{N}\equiv 1
.

Lemma 13.6
.

For any
u\in\mathbb{R}
and any
t>0
, the identity

(13.34)

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\ee^{-tk+u}}{(1+\ee^{-tk+u})^{2}}=\frac{1}{t}+\frac{8\pi}{t^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{k\cos(2\pi ku/t)}{\ee^{2\pi^{2}k/t}-\ee^{-2\pi^{2}k/t}}

holds true.

Proof.

Setting

f(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{\ee^{-tx+u}}{(1+\ee^{-tx+u})^{2}},\quad\text{with Fourier transform}\quad\widehat{f}(w)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}f(x)\ee^{-2\pi\mathrm{i}wx}\mathrm{d}x,

Poisson Summation Formula yields the identity

(13.35)

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\ee^{-tk+u}}{(1+\ee^{-tk+u})^{2}}=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}f(k)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{f}(k)=\widehat{f}(0)+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(\widehat{f}(k)+\widehat{f}(-k)).

Through the change of variables
x\mapsto y=tx-u
, we obtain

\widehat{f}(w)=\frac{\ee^{-2\pi\mathrm{i}wu/t}}{t}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{\ee^{y}}{(1+\ee^{y})^{2}}\ee^{-2\pi\mathrm{i}wy/t}\mathrm{d}t.

For
w=0
, this integral equals
1
. For
w\neq 0
, we use the identity

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{\ee^{y}}{(1+\ee^{y})^{2}}\ee^{-\mathrm{i}\omega y}\mathrm{d}y=\frac{2\omega}{\ee^{2\pi\omega}-\ee^{-2\pi\omega}},

which is the Fourier transform of the logistic distribution. Replacing these identities in (
13.35
), we obtain the result.
∎

Remark 13.7
.

The
4
-th Jacobi Theta function
\theta_{4}(z{\,|\,}q)
satisfies the identity
[
1
, Equation (20.5.13)]

\partial_{z}\log\theta_{4}(z{\,|\,}q)=4\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{q^{n}}{1-q^{2n}}\sin(2nz).

Differentiating this identity one more time and using the result into (
13.34
), we obtain the alternative representation

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\ee^{-tk+u}}{(1+\ee^{-tk+u})^{2}}=\frac{1}{t}+\frac{\pi}{t}\partial_{z}^{2}\log\theta_{4}(z{\,|\,}q)\Bigg|_{z=\pi u/t,\,q=\ee^{-2\pi^{2}/t}},

which provides a more compact way of expressing (
13.35
).

Let us briefly describe how we will proceed to estimate
\mathsf{S}(S,s)
. The term

\frac{\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v}}{(1+\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v})^{2}}=\frac{\ee^{-Nt|\frac{k}{N}-z_{N}(v)|}}{(1+\ee^{-Nt|\frac{k}{N}-z_{N}(v)|})^{2}}

has non-negligible mass for
k/N
around
z_{N}(v)
, and it decays exponentially fast to zero elsewhere. Thus, at the formal level, we expect that the major contribution should be coming from this point
\frac{k}{N}\approx z_{N}(v)
, corresponding to
\zeta\approx\zeta_{N}(v)
. Near these points,

\varphi^{\prime}\left(\frac{k}{N}\right)=-\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{P}}+\mathit{o}(1)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{H}_{N}(\zeta-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}{\,|\,}s)=\mathcal{H}_{N}(0{\,|\,}s)+\mathit{o}(1).

As we will show later, such heuristics will in turn imply that

\sum_{k\in N\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}}\varphi^{\prime}\left(\frac{k}{N}\right)\mathcal{H}_{N}\left(\zeta\left(\frac{k}{N}\right)-\zeta_{N}(v)\Big|\,v\right)\frac{\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v}}{(1+\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v})^{2}}\\
\approx-\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}\mathcal{H}_{N}(0{\,|\,}v)\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v}}{(1+\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v})^{2}}\mathrm{d}v.

The remaining sum is then computed exactly through (
13.6
) and the choice
u=Nt\mathsf{a}-v
, and it yields that

\mathsf{S}(S,s)\approx\\
\frac{\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}}{N^{1/3}2\pi\mathrm{i}t}\left[\int_{s}^{S}\mathcal{H}_{N}(0{\,|\,}v)\mathrm{d}v+\frac{8\pi}{t}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{k}{\ee^{2\pi^{2}k/t}-\ee^{-2\pi^{2}k/t}}\int_{s}^{S}\mathcal{H}_{N}(0{\,|\,}v)\cos\left(\frac{2\pi k}{t}(Nt\mathsf{a}-v)\right)\mathrm{d}v\right].

In line with the classical stationary phase method for estimation of oscillatory integrals, we then show that the integrals of
\mathcal{H}_{N}(0{\,|\,}v)
against the cosine terms are of smaller order than the integral of
\mathcal{H}_{N}(0{\,|\,}v)
itself. Furthermore, with the asymptotics for
\mathcal{H}_{N}(0{\,|\,}v)
that we already have, we then also analyze this leading integral asymptotically, which ultimately will yield the leading term asymptotics for
\mathsf{S}(S,s)
- in fact, we will introduce these asymptotics for
\mathsf{H}_{N}
even before turning this last approximation into a rigorous estimate.

However, to turn this outline into rigorous results, we once again need to split the analysis into the subcritical, critical and supercritical cases, as in each of these cases we have different asymptotic behavior for
\mathcal{H}_{N}
. We now proceed to the details.

13.3.1.
Analysis of
\mathsf{S}
in the subcritical regime

The asymptotic behavior of
\mathcal{H}_{N}
in the subcritical regime is described by Theorem
9.2
–(i). We use it and rewrite (
13.33
) as

(13.36)

\mathsf{S}(S,s)=-\frac{1}{N}\int_{s}^{S}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}}\zeta^{\prime}\left(x\right)\left[\mathsf{A}\left(\zeta\left(x\right),\zeta\left(x\right)\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-\eta\zeta_{N}(v)-\frac{4}{3}\zeta(x)^{3/2}_{+}}\right)\right]\frac{\ee^{N^{1/3}\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta(x){\,|\,}v)}}{\left(1+\ee^{N^{1/3}\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta(x){\,|\,}v)}\right)^{2}}\mathrm{d}v,

valid as
N\to\infty
. Based on the heuristics that the major contribution should be coming from points
\zeta(x)\approx\zeta_{N}(v)
, and as we will see also guided by the asymptotc behavior of
\mathsf{A}
in (
2.55
) and (
2.56
), for fixed
\delta>0
we introduce the sets

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{<}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}{\,|\,}\zeta(x)\leq 0\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{0}=\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{0}(v)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}{\,|\,}|\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(v)|<\delta\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{>}=\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{>}(v)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}{\,|\,}0\leq\zeta(x)\leq\zeta_{N}(v)-\delta\text{ or }\zeta\geq\zeta_{N}(v)+\delta\},

and split

\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}}\zeta^{\prime}\left(x\right)\left[\mathsf{A}\left(\zeta\left(x\right),\zeta\left(x\right)\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-\eta\zeta_{N}(v)-\frac{4}{3}\zeta(x)^{3/2}_{+}}\right)\right]\frac{\ee^{N^{1/3}\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta(x){\,|\,}v)}}{\left(1+\ee^{N^{1/3}\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta(x){\,|\,}v)}\right)^{2}}=S_{<}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}+S_{0}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}+S_{>}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})},

where
S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{j}
corresponds to the sum over
\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{j}
,
j=0,<,>
.

Recall that
\zeta(x),\zeta^{\prime}(x)=\mathcal{O}(N^{2/3})
uniformly for
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
. In the subcritical regime, we have that
\zeta_{N}(v)
is sufficiently large (and) positive, and therefore using Proposition
6.9
we see that the exponential factor
\ee^{\bullet}/(1+\ee^{\bullet})^{2}
in the sum is
\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta N^{1/3}|\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)|})
. From (
2.56
), the Airy factor is
\mathcal{O}(\zeta(x)^{1/2})=\mathcal{O}(N^{1/3})
. All in all, we obtain

S_{<}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{<}}\mathcal{O}\left(N\ee^{-\eta^{\prime}N^{1/3}|\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(v)|}\right)=\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-\eta N^{1/3}\zeta_{N}(v)}\right),\quad N\to\infty

where for the last equality we used that
|\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)|=|\zeta|+\zeta_{N}(v)
because
\zeta_{N}(v)>0
in the subcritical regime, and also that
\mathcal{X}_{<}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}\subset\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
has
\mathcal{O}(N)
elements. From the very definition of the subcritical regime in Assumptions
2.7
and the expansion (
6.6
) with
\tau=N^{1/3}
, we see that

(13.37)

N^{1/3}\zeta_{N}(v)\geq N^{1/3}\gg\zeta_{N}(v)^{3/2},\quad N\to\infty,

and we update the last estimate to

(13.38)

S_{<}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{<}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}}\mathcal{O}\left(N\ee^{-\eta N^{1/3}|\zeta(x)|-\eta N^{1/3}\zeta_{N}(v)}\right)=\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-3\zeta_{N}(v)^{3/2}}\right),

which will be more useful for us later. The constant
3
is chosen above solely for notational convenience, any constant strictly larger than
4/3
would work for what comes next, with suitable adjustments of other constants appearing later on.

Next, we estimate the sum over
\mathcal{X}_{>}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
. In this set, we estimate the factor
\mathsf{A}
simply as a bounded factor. Using Proposition
6.9
we then estimate

(13.39)

S_{>}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{<}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}}\mathcal{O}\left(N^{2/3}\ee^{-\eta N^{1/3}|\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)|}\right)=\mathcal{O}\left(N^{\frac{5}{3}}\ee^{-N^{1/3}\eta\delta}\right)=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-3\zeta_{N}(v)^{3/2}}),

where in the last estimate we used again (
13.37
).

Lastly, we need to estimate the sum over
\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{0}
. We assume that
s
is sufficiently large so that the asymptotics (
2.55
) are valid for every
\zeta(x)\in\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}(v)
and any
v\geq s
. This way, we estimate immediately

\mathsf{A}(\zeta(x),\zeta(x))+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\zeta_{N}(v)-\frac{4}{3}\zeta(x)_{+}^{3/2}})=\mathsf{A}(\zeta(x),\zeta(x))\left(1+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta^{\prime}\zeta_{N}(v)})\right),\quad x\in\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})},\quad N\to\infty,

where, as always, this estimate is valid uniformly for
s,S
in the subcritical regime.

To continue, let us fix
\nu\in(0,1/4)
and further split

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}=\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}(v)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{0}{\,|\,}\frac{\delta}{N^{\nu}\zeta_{N}(v)^{1/2}}\leq|\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(v)|<\delta\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}=\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(v)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{0}{\,|\,}|\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(v)|<\frac{\delta}{N^{\nu}\zeta_{N}(v)^{1/2}}\right\},

with corresponding split of sum
S_{0}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}=S_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}+S_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
with

S_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}=\left(1+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\zeta_{N}(v)})\right)\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{j}}\zeta^{\prime}(x)\mathsf{A}(\zeta(x),\zeta(x))\frac{\ee^{N^{1/3}\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta(x))}}{(1+\ee^{N^{1/3}\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta(x))})^{2}},\quad j=({\rm in}),({\rm out}).

Along
\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
, we use again Proposition
6.9
and (
2.55
) to estimate

S_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}}\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta(x)^{3/2}-2\eta N^{1/3}|\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(v)|}\right).

It is simple to see that
\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}\subset\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
, where the latter set was introduced in (
13.11
). Following the same arguments carried out in (
13.14
)–(
13.15
), we see that the minimum of the function
\zeta\mapsto\frac{4}{3}\zeta^{3/2}+\eta N^{1/3}|\zeta-\zeta_{N}(v)|
on
[\zeta_{N}(v)-\delta,\zeta_{N}(v)+\delta]\supset\zeta(\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})})
is attained at
\zeta=\zeta_{N}(v)
. This argument yields the bound

\displaystyle S_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}}\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta_{N}(v)^{3/2}-\eta N^{1/3}|\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(v)|}\right)=\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta_{N}(v)^{3/2}}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}}\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta N^{1/3}|\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(v)|})

(13.40)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}\zeta_{N}(v)^{3/2}-\eta^{\prime}N^{1/3-\nu}\zeta_{N}(v)^{-1/2}}).

We notice that because we are in the subcritical regime, we have
N^{1/3-\nu}\zeta_{N}(v)^{-1/2}\geq\delta N^{1/4-\nu}
, so the corresponding term in the estimate above decays exponentially with a power of
N
.

Finally, we now want to estimate the sum over
\mathcal{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
, which will yield the leading contribution. From the very definition of
\mathcal{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
, the estimate

\zeta(x)^{3/2}=\zeta_{N}(v)^{3/2}\left(1-\frac{\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(v)}{\zeta_{N}(v)}\right)^{3/2}=\zeta_{N}(v)^{3/2}+\mathcal{O}\left(\zeta_{N}^{1/2}(\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N})\right)=\zeta_{N}(v)^{3/2}+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\nu})

is valid as
N\to\infty
uniformly for
x\in\mathcal{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}
. This estimate combined with (
2.55
) yields

\mathsf{A}(\zeta(x),\zeta(x))=\mathsf{A}(\zeta_{N}(v),\zeta_{N}(v))\left(1+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\nu})\right),\quad N\to\infty,

also uniformly for
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
. Also, by the analyticity of
x\mapsto\zeta=N^{2/3}\varphi(x)
,

(13.41)

\frac{C}{N^{2/3}}|\zeta_{N}(v)-\zeta(x)|\leq|x-z_{N}(v)|\leq\frac{C}{N^{2/3}}|\zeta_{N}(v)-\zeta(x)|

and therefore we estimate
\zeta^{\prime}(x)=-N^{2/3}\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}(1+\mathcal{O}(N^{-2/3-\nu}\zeta_{N}(v)^{-1/2}))
. Hence,

S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}=-\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}N^{2/3}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\zeta_{N}(v)}+N^{-\nu})\right)\mathsf{A}(\zeta_{N}(v),\zeta_{N}(v))\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}}\frac{\ee^{N^{1/3}\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta(x))}}{(1+\ee^{N^{1/3}\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta(x))})^{2}}.

Writing
N^{1/3}\mathsf{P}_{N}(\zeta(x))=-Nt(x-z_{N}(v))
, and using the first inequality in (
13.41
), we see that the sum above may be extended to a sum over the whole set
\frac{N}{\mathbb{Z}}
, at the cost of an error of order at most
\ee^{-\eta N^{1/4-\nu}}
. Therefore,

(13.42)

S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}=-\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}N^{2/3}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\zeta_{N}(v)}+N^{-\nu})\right)\mathsf{A}(\zeta_{N}(v),\zeta_{N}(v))\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v}}{(1+\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v})^{2}},

valid as
N\to\infty
, uniformly for
v\in[s,S]
and
s\leq S
in the subcritical regime.

Finally, using (
2.55
) we compare (
13.38
), (
13.39
), (
13.40
) and (
13.42
) asymptotically, concluding that the dominant term is precisely
S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sb})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
. Plugging this info into (
13.36
), we obtain in summary that

\mathsf{S}(S,s)=\frac{\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}}{N^{1/3}}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta\zeta_{N}(s)}+N^{-\nu})\right)\int_{s}^{S}\mathsf{A}(\zeta_{N}(v),\zeta_{N}(v))\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v}}{(1+\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v})^{2}}\mathrm{d}v.

Using once more (
2.55
) and the expansion (
6.6
) for
\zeta_{N}
, we see that

\mathsf{A}(\zeta_{N}(v),\zeta_{N}(v))=\mathsf{A}\left(\frac{v}{\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{P}}N^{1/3}},\frac{v}{\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{P}}N^{1/3}}\right)\left(1+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{v^{5/2}}{N^{3/2}}\right)\right).

In the subcritical regime this last error term is of order at least
N^{-1/6}
, and therefore

\mathsf{S}(S,s)=\frac{\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}}{N^{1/3}}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta s/N^{1/3}}+N^{-\nu})\right)\int_{s}^{S}\mathsf{A}\left(\frac{v}{\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{P}}N^{1/3}},\frac{v}{\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{P}}N^{1/3}}\right)\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v}}{(1+\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v})^{2}}\mathrm{d}v,

valid as
N\to\infty
, for any
\nu\in(0,1/6)
and uniformly for
s,S
in the subcritical regime.

Having in mind that
\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{P}}=t/\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}
(see (
11.26
)), using Lemma
13.6
, changing variables

(13.43)

v=\frac{tN^{1/3}y}{\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}}

recalling the change
s\mapsto s_{N},S\mapsto S_{N}
from (
13.32
) and setting for simplicity

(13.44)

d_{k}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{8\pi}{t}\frac{k}{\ee^{2\pi^{2}kt}-\ee^{-2\pi^{2}kt}},\quad k\geq 1,\

we obtain

\mathsf{S}(S,s)=\left(1+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta s/N^{1/3}}+N^{-\nu})\right)\\
\times\left[\int_{s_{N}}^{S_{N}}\mathsf{A}(y,y)\mathrm{d}y+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}d_{k}\int_{s_{N}}^{S_{N}}\mathsf{A}(y,y)\cos\left(2\pi k(Nt\mathsf{a}-\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}^{-1}N^{1/3}y)\right)\mathrm{d}y\right].

Using (
2.55
), a simple application of Laplace’s method for asymptotics of integrals shows that

(13.45)

\int_{t}^{\infty}\mathsf{A}(y,y)\mathrm{d}y=\frac{1}{16\pi}\frac{\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}t^{3/2}}}{t^{3/2}}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(t^{-3/2})\right),\quad t\to\infty,

and we apply this estimate for
t=s_{n}
and
t=S_{N}
. In turn, integration by parts and again an application of Laplace’s method yields

\int_{s_{N}}^{S_{N}}\mathsf{A}(y,y)\cos\left(2\pi k(Nt\mathsf{a}-\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}^{-1}N^{1/3}y)\right)\mathrm{d}y=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\ee^{-\frac{4}{3}s_{N}^{3/2}}}{kN^{1/3}s_{N}}\right),\quad N\to\infty,

both being valid uniformly for
s<S
in the subcritical regime. This way, and using in addition that
sN^{-1/3}\ll N^{1/6}
to suppress a further error term when comparing these last two terms, and we conclude the following result.

Theorem 13.8
.

For any
\nu\in(0,1/6)
, the estimate

\mathsf{S}(s,S)=\left(1+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta s/N^{1/3}}+N^{-\nu})\right)\int_{s_{N}}^{S_{N}}\mathsf{A}(y,y)\mathrm{d}y,\quad N\to\infty,

is valid uniformly for
s<S
and both in the subcritical regime.

13.3.2.
Analysis of
\mathsf{S}
in the critical regime

We start recalling that in the critical regime, the asymptotic behavior of
\mathcal{H}_{N}
is described by Theorem
9.2
–(ii). Based on the asymptotic behavior described therein, we fix
L>0
and introduce the sets

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{0}=\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{0}(v)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}{\,|\,}|\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(v)|\leq L\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{<}=\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{<}(v)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}{\,|\,}\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(v)<-L\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{>}=\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{>}(v)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}{\,|\,}\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(v)>L\}.

For what comes next, we also recall that
\zeta_{N}(v)
remains bounded for
v
in the critical regime. In particular, for any
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
we have
|\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(v)|\leq\kappa N^{2/3}
for some constant
\kappa>0
(see (
13.8
)).

We express the sum on the first line of (
13.33
) as

\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}}\zeta^{\prime}(x)\mathcal{H}_{N}(\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(v){\,|\,}v)\frac{\ee^{-Nt(x-z_{N}(v))}}{(1+\ee^{-Nt(x-z_{N}(v))})^{2}}=S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{0}+S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{<}+S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{>},

where the term
S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{j}
corresponds to the sum over
\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{j}=\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{j}(v)
,
j=0,<,>
.

To estimate each of these sums, observe that the very definition of
\zeta=N^{2/3}\varphi
and (
11.22
) imply that there exist
c,C>0
such that

cN^{2/3}|x-\mathsf{a}|\leq|\zeta(x)|\leq CN^{2/3}|x-\mathsf{a}|\quad\text{as well as}\quad cN^{2/3}\leq|\zeta^{\prime}(x)|\leq CN^{2/3}.

Consequently, in the critical regime,

\displaystyle|x-\mathsf{a}|-\frac{M}{N^{2/3}}\leq|x-\mathsf{a}|-|x-z_{N}(v)|\leq|x-z_{N}(v)|\leq|x-\mathsf{a}|+|x-z_{N}(v)|\leq|x-\mathsf{a}|+\frac{M}{N^{2/3}},

as well as

cN^{2/3}|x-\mathsf{a}|-\widetilde{M}\leq|\zeta(x)|-|\zeta_{N}(v)|\leq|\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(v)|\leq|\zeta(x)|+|\zeta_{N}(v)|\leq CN^{2/3}|x-\mathsf{a}|+\widetilde{M}

for some
M,\widetilde{M}>0
.
These estimates together with Theorem
9.2
–(ii) imply that

S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{>}=\mathcal{O}\left(N^{2/3}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{>}}\ee^{-\eta N^{1/3}|\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(v)|}\ee^{-NC|x-z_{N}(v)|}\right)=\mathcal{O}(N^{1/3}\ee^{-\eta N^{2/3}L}|\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{>}|),

for yet new constants
\eta,C>0
. Now, the set
\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}\supset\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{<}
has
\mathcal{O}(N)
elements, and from the estimate above we conclude that

(13.46)

S_{>}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta N^{1/3}}),\quad N\to\infty,

for some constant
\eta>0
, uniformly for
v
in the critical regime.

In a completely similar way,

(13.47)

S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{<}=\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta N^{1/3}}),\quad N\to\infty,

uniformly for
v
in the critical regime.

We now estimate the sum
S_{0}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}
. Observe that
|x-\mathsf{a}|=\mathcal{O}(N^{-2/3})
for
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{0}
, and therefore

\zeta^{\prime}(x)=N^{2/3}\varphi^{\prime}(x)=-N^{2/3}\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}(1+\mathcal{O}(N^{-2/3})),

where we used (
11.22
), and where the error term is independent of
v
. Combining with Theorem
9.2
–(ii), we obtain

S_{0}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}=-2\pi\mathrm{i}\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}N^{2/3}\left(1+\mathcal{O}(N^{-2/3})\right)\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{0}}\mathsf{K}_{{\rm P34}}(\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(v),\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(v){\,|\,}\zeta_{N}(v))\frac{\ee^{-Nt(x-z_{N}(v))}}{(1+\ee^{-Nt(x-z_{N}(v))})^{2}},

valid for
N\to\infty
, again with error term uniform for
v
in the positive critical regime. Next, for any fixed
\nu\in(0,1/3)
we split the set
\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}
into two further subsets,

\mathcal{X}_{0}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}=\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}\cup\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})},

with

\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}=\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{0}{\,|\,}|x-z_{N}(v)|\leq N^{-2/3-\nu}\},\quad\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}=\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{0}{\,|\,}|x-z_{N}(v)|\geq N^{-2/3-\nu}\}.

The term
\mathsf{K}_{\rm P34}
is bounded on
\mathcal{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}
, and from the very definition of
\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
we obtain that the corresponding sum over this set is
\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta N^{1/3-\nu}})
, for some
\eta>0
. On the other hand,
|\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(v)|=\mathcal{O}(N^{-\nu})
for
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
, and the analyticity of
\mathsf{K}_{\rm P34}(\zeta,\zeta{\,|\,}y)
as a function of
\zeta
implies that

\mathsf{K}_{{\rm P34}}(\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(v),\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(v){\,|\,}\zeta_{N}(v))=\mathsf{K}_{{\rm P34}}\left(0,0{\,|\,}\zeta_{N}(v)\right)(1+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\nu}),\quad N\to\infty,

again valid uniformly for
v
in the critical regime. All in all, we obtain

S_{0}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}=-2\pi\mathrm{i}N^{2/3}\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}\mathsf{K}_{{\rm P34}}\left(0,0{\,|\,}\zeta_{N}(v)\right)(1+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\nu}))\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}}\frac{\ee^{-Nt(x-z_{N}(v))}}{(1+\ee^{-Nt(x-z_{N}(v))})^{2}},

valid as
N\to\infty
, for any
\nu\in(0,1/3)
, uniformly for
v
in the critical regime. The sum over
\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm c})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
above may be replaced by a sum over the whole set
\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}
, at a cost of an exponentially small error. Consequently, combining this last estimate with (
13.46
) and (
13.47
) into (
13.33
), we conclude

\mathsf{S}(S,s)=\frac{\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}}{N^{1/3}}(1+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\nu}))\int_{s}^{S}\mathsf{K}_{\rm P34}(0,0{\,|\,}\zeta_{N}(v))\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v}}{(1+\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v})^{2}}\mathrm{d}v,

where the error term is valid uniformly for
s<S
and both in the critical regime, and where the error is valid for any
\nu\in(0,1/3)
. Now, for
s,S
in the critical regime we have
|v|\leq CN^{1/3}
in the integration interval. From the analyticity of
y\mapsto\mathsf{K}_{\rm P34}(0,0{\,|\,}y)
and the expansion (
6.6
), we expand

\mathsf{K}_{\rm P34}(0,0{\,|\,}\zeta_{N}(v))=\mathsf{K}_{\rm P34}\left(0,0{\,|\,}\frac{v}{\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{P}}N^{1/3}}\right)\left(1+\mathcal{O}(N^{-2/3})\right),

uniformly for
v
in the critical regime. Recalling that
\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{P}}=t/\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}
(see again (
11.26
)), we update the last estimate on
\mathsf{S}(S,s)
to

\mathsf{S}(S,s)=\frac{\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}}{N^{1/3}}(1+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\nu}))\int_{s}^{S}\mathsf{K}_{\rm P34}\left(0,0{\,|\,}\frac{\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}v}{tN^{1/3}}\right)\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v}}{(1+\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v})^{2}}\mathrm{d}v,

Changing variables
v=tN^{1/3}y/\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}
as in (
13.43
)
et seq.
, we update this estimate to

\mathsf{S}(S,s)=\left(1+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\nu})\right)\\
\times\left[\int_{s_{N}}^{S_{N}}\mathsf{K}_{{\rm P34}}(0,0{\,|\,}y)\mathrm{d}y+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}d_{k}\int_{s_{N}}^{S_{N}}\mathsf{K}_{{\rm P34}}(0,0{\,|\,}y)\cos\left(2\pi k(Nt\mathsf{a}-\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}^{-1}N^{1/3}y)\right)\mathrm{d}y\right].

where we stress that
s_{N},S_{N}
are as in (
13.32
). In the critical regime both
S_{N}
and
s_{N}
remain bounded. Because
\partial_{y}\mathsf{K}_{\rm P34}(0,0{\,|\,}y)
is bounded on bounded subsets of the real line (see (
1.21
)), a simple integration by parts shows now that

\int_{s_{N}}^{S_{N}}\mathsf{K}_{{\rm P34}}(0,0{\,|\,}y)\cos\left(2\pi k(Nt\mathsf{a}-\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}^{-1}N^{1/3}y)\right)\mathrm{d}y=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{kN^{1/3}}\right),

uniformly in
s,S
, and we finally concluded the following.

Theorem 13.9
.

For any
\nu\in(0,1/3)
, the estimate

\mathsf{S}(S,s)=\left(1+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\nu})\right)\int_{s_{N}}^{S_{N}}\mathsf{K}_{{\rm P34}}\left(0,0{\,|\,}y\right)\mathrm{d}y,\quad N\to\infty,

is valid uniformly for
s<S
in the critical regime.

We finally move to the last case, namely the supercritical regime.

13.3.3.
Analysis of
\mathsf{S}
in the supercritical regime

The asymptotic behavior of
\mathcal{H}_{N}
in the supercritical regime is described by Theorem
9.2
–(iii). Based on the estimates therein, we fix
\delta>0
and set

\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}=\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}{\,|\,}|\zeta-{\zeta_{N}(s)}|\leq\delta\},

and

S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}=S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}}\zeta^{\prime}(x)\mathcal{H}_{N}(\zeta(x)-{\zeta_{N}(s)}{\,|\,}v)\frac{\ee^{-Nt(x-{z_{N}(s)})}}{(1+\ee^{-Nt(x-{z_{N}(s)})})^{2}}.

We insert the first estimate in Theorem
9.2
–(iii), obtaining in a straightforward manner that the estimate

(13.48)

\mathsf{S}(S,s)=-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}N}\int_{s}^{S}S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(v)\mathrm{d}v+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta N^{1/3}}),\quad N\to\infty,

is valid uniformly for
s,S
in the supercritical regime. To analyze the withstanding term, recall the chain of change of variables

x\mapsto\zeta=\zeta(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=N^{2/3}\varphi(x),\qquad\text{and}\qquad\zeta\mapsto\xi=\xi(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}={\zeta_{N}(s)}^{2}\zeta,

which induces the change of variables

x\mapsto\xi(x)=\xi(\zeta(x))=N^{2/3}{\zeta_{N}(s)}^{2}\varphi(x),\quad\text{so that}\quad\xi_{N}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\zeta({\zeta_{N}(s)})={\zeta_{N}(s)}^{3}.

We use this change of variables extensively in the coming calculations.

Let us split
\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}=\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}\cup\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
, with

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}{\,|\,}|\xi(x)-\xi_{N}(s)|\leq\frac{|{\zeta_{N}(s)}|}{N^{1/6}}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}{\,|\,}\frac{|{\zeta_{N}(s)}|}{N^{1/6}}<|\xi(x)-\xi_{N}(s)|\leq{\zeta_{N}(s)}^{2}\delta\right\}.

This split of the set
\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
induces a split
S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}=S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}+S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}
, and we now estimate each of these terms. For these estimates, we notice that
|{\zeta_{N}(s)}|/N^{1/6}=\mathcal{O}(|s|/N^{1/2})
is arbitrarily small for
s
in the supercritical regime (see (
6.6
)), and once again we recall Theorem
9.2
–(iii), which will guide our arguments.

We start from
S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
. Estimates (
2.58
)–(
2.59
) give us the rough bound

\mathsf{J}_{0}(-(\xi-\xi_{N}(s)),-(\xi-\xi_{N}(s)))=\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{2(\xi-\xi_{N}(s))^{1/2}_{+}}\right),\quad\xi=\xi(x)\in\mathcal{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}.

Furthermore,
\zeta^{\prime}(x)=\mathcal{O}(N^{2/3})
uniformly for
x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
. Combining these estimates with Theorem
9.2
–(iii), we obtain that for some
\eta>0
, independent of
s
, the estimate

S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}=\mathcal{O}\left(N^{2/3}{\zeta_{N}(s)}^{2}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}}\ee^{2(\xi(x)-\xi_{N}(s))^{1/2}_{+}-3\eta N^{1/3}|\zeta-{\zeta_{N}(s)}|}\right),

is valid uniformly for
s
in the supercritical regime.

Let us look at the exponent of the exponential in the sum. For
\xi(x)-\xi_{N}(s)<0
, we have
(\xi(x)-\xi_{N}(x))^{1/2}_{+}=0
and clearly the exponential in the sum is bounded by
\ee^{-2\eta N^{1/3}|\zeta-{\zeta_{N}(s)}|}
. For
\xi(x)-\xi_{N}(s)\geq 0
, we write (part of the) exponent as

(13.49)

-2(\xi(x)-\xi_{N}(s))_{+}^{1/2}+\eta N^{1/3}|\zeta-{\zeta_{N}(s)}|=-2u+\frac{\eta N^{1/3}}{{\zeta_{N}(s)}^{2}}u^{2},\quad u=(\xi(x)-\xi_{N}(s))^{1/2}.

A calculus exercise shows that this function of
u
attains its minimum value on the positive axis at the point
u_{c}={\zeta_{N}(s)}^{2}/(\eta N^{1/3})
, and evaluating at this point we conclude that

-2(\xi(x)-\xi_{N}(s))_{+}^{1/2}+\eta N^{1/3}|\zeta-{\zeta_{N}(s)}|\geq-u_{c}=-{\zeta_{N}(s)}^{2}/(\eta N^{1/3})

This latter value is bounded from below uniformly for
s
in the supercritical regime. As a consequence, we obtain that

S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}=\mathcal{O}\left(N^{2/3}{\zeta_{N}(s)}^{2}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}}\ee^{-2\eta N^{1/3}|\zeta-{\zeta_{N}(s)}|}\right).

On the set
\mathcal{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}
, we bound

N^{1/3}|\zeta(x)-{\zeta_{N}(s)}|=\frac{N^{1/3}}{{\zeta_{N}(s)}^{2}}|\xi(x)-\xi_{N}(s)|\geq\frac{N^{1/6}}{|{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|}.

Similarly, we also have
|\zeta(x)-\zeta_{N}(s)|\geq cN^{2/3}|x-{z_{N}(s)}|
, for some
c>0
, and therefore

S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}=\mathcal{O}\left(N^{2/3}{\zeta_{N}(s)}^{2}\ee^{-\eta N^{1/6}/|{\zeta_{N}(s)}|}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm out})}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}}\ee^{-\eta cN|x-{z_{N}(s)}|}\right).

Writing
x=k/N
, it is straightforward to show that the remaining integral is finite. Our findings so far are summarized as

(13.50)

S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s)=S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(s)+\mathcal{O}\left(N^{2/3}{\zeta_{N}(s)}^{2}\ee^{-\eta N^{1/6}/{\zeta_{N}(s)}}\right),\quad N\to\infty,

uniformly for
s
in the supercritical regime, and where we stress that
\eta>0
is independent of
s
.

Finally, we now analyze the withstanding term
S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
. In this set, for some
C>0
,

\displaystyle|x-\mathsf{a}|

\displaystyle\leq|{z_{N}(s)}-\mathsf{a}|+|x-{z_{N}(s)}|\leq\frac{|s|}{tN}+\frac{C}{N^{2/3}}|\zeta(x)-{\zeta_{\tau}(s)}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|s|}{tN}+C\frac{{\zeta_{N}(s)}^{2}}{N^{2/3}}|\xi(x)-\xi_{N}(s)|\leq\frac{|s|}{tN}+\frac{C}{|{\zeta_{N}(s)}|N^{5/6}}.

The constant
C>0
depends only on the conformal map
x\mapsto\varphi(x)
, hence it is independent of
s
. In particular, this inequality shows that
|x-\mathsf{a}|=\mathcal{O}(s/N)
, uniformly for
s
in the supercritical regime and
x\in\mathcal{S}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
, and therefore

\zeta^{\prime}(x)=-N^{2/3}\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}\left(1+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{s}{N}\right)\right),\quad x\in\mathcal{X}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})},\quad N\to\infty,

again uniformly for
s
in the supercritical regime.

Using (
2.58
), we see that

\mathsf{J}_{0}(-(\xi-\xi_{N}(s)),-(\xi-\xi_{N}(s)))=\frac{1}{4}\left(1+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{s}{N^{1/2}}\right)\right),\quad N\to\infty,

uniformly for
\xi\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
and
s
in the supercritical regime. With the asymptotics given by Theorem
9.2
–(iii), we obtain

(13.51)

S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}(s)=-N^{2/3}{\zeta_{N}(s)}^{2}\frac{\pi\mathrm{i}\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}}{2}\left(1+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{s}{N^{1/2}}\right)\right)\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}}\frac{\ee^{-Nt|x-{z_{N}(s)}|}}{(1+\ee^{-Nt|x-{z_{N}(s)}|})^{2}}\\
+\mathcal{O}\left(N^{2/3}\left(|{\zeta_{N}(s)}|+\frac{{\zeta_{N}(s)}^{4}}{N^{1/3}}\right)\right)\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}}\frac{\ee^{-Nt|x-{z_{N}(s)}|+2(\xi(x)-\xi_{N}(s))_{+}^{1/2}}}{(1+\ee^{-Nt|x-{z_{N}(s)}|})^{2}},

The same arguments provided in (
13.49
)
et seq.
show that the sum on the second line above is finite. As for the first sum, we bound as before
Nt|x-{z_{N}(s)}|/2\geq\eta\frac{N^{1/3}}{{\zeta_{N}(s)}^{2}}|\xi-\xi_{N}(s)|\geq\eta N^{1/6}/|{\zeta_{N}(s)}|
for
x\in\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}\setminus\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}
and a constant
\eta>0
independent of
s
, obtaining as a consequence

\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}_{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}}\frac{\ee^{-Nt|x-{z_{N}(s)}|}}{(1+\ee^{-Nt|x-{z_{N}(s)}|})^{2}}=\sum_{x\in\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\ee^{-Nt|x-{z_{N}(s)}|}}{(1+\ee^{-Nt|x-{z_{N}(s)}|})^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-\eta N^{1/6}/|{\zeta_{N}(s)}|^{1/6}}\right),\quad N\to\infty.

Using these bounds in (
13.51
) and returning the resulting asymptotic expression in (
13.50
), we obtain

(13.52)

S^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm sp})}(s)=\\
-N^{2/3}{\zeta_{N}(s)}^{2}\frac{\pi\mathrm{i}\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}}{2}\left(1+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{s}{N^{1/2}}\right)\right)\sum_{x\in\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\ee^{-Nt|x-{z_{N}(s)}|}}{(1+\ee^{-Nt|x-{z_{N}(s)}|})^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(N^{2/3}|{\zeta_{N}(s)}|+N^{1/3}{\zeta_{N}(s)}^{4}\right),

which is valid as
N\to\infty
, uniformly for
s
in the supercritical regime.

There are three error terms in the expression above, one of order
\mathcal{O}(N^{2/3}{\zeta_{N}(s)}^{2}s/N^{1/2})
coming in front of the sum (which is finite), and the remaining two on the right-most side. A direct calculation shows that, in the supercritical regime, the first one is dominant over the latter two. With this fact in mind, we use this estimate in (
13.48
), obtaining

\mathsf{S}(S,s)=\frac{\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}}{4N^{1/3}}\int_{s}^{S}\zeta_{N}(v)^{2}\left(1+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{v}{N^{1/2}}\right)\right)\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v}}{(1+\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v})^{2}}\mathrm{d}v,

where we parametrized the sum as
x=k/N
. Thanks to (
6.6
) and again the identity
\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{P}}=t/\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}
, we rewrite this estimate as

\mathsf{S}(S,s)=\frac{\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}^{3}}{4t^{2}N}\int_{s}^{S}v^{2}\left(1+\frac{v}{N^{1/2}}\right)\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v}}{(1+\ee^{-tk+Nt\mathsf{a}-v})^{2}}\mathrm{d}v.

The remaining integrals could be expressed in terms of logarithms and polylogarithms. Instead, we follow the same route as before and apply (
13.34
), obtaining

\mathsf{S}(S,s)=\frac{\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}^{3}}{4t^{3}N}\left[\int_{s}^{S}v^{2}\left(1+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{v}{N^{1/2}}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}v+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}d_{k}\int_{s}^{S}v^{2}\left(1+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{v}{N^{1/2}}\right)\right)\cos\left(2\pi k(Na-t^{-1}v)\right)\mathrm{d}v\right],

valid as
N\to\infty
, uniformly for
s\leq S
in the supercritical regime.
A direct integration shows that the
k
-th (indefinite) integral inside the series is
\mathcal{O}(v^{2}/k)
. In the supercritical regime, this contribution is smaller in order than the contribution
\mathcal{O}(v^{4}/N^{1/2})
arising from the error term in the first integral. We concluded

Theorem 13.10
.

The estimate

\mathsf{S}(S,s)=\frac{\mathsf{c}_{\varphi}^{3}}{12t^{3}N}(S^{3}-s^{3})+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{S^{4}-s^{4}}{N^{3/2}}\right)

is valid uniformly for
s\leq S
in the supercritical regime.

Observe that the leading contribution and the error term become of the same order precisely when
s=\mathcal{O}(N^{1/2})
.

This result ends our asymptotic analysis, and we now collect all the ingredients to complete the proof of our main results.

14.
Proof of main results on dOPEs

We are finally ready to conclude the proof of our main results on discrete orthogonal polynomial ensembles.

14.1.
Proof of Theorem
2.8

Our starting point is (
2.31
). With the help of (
2.29
) and (
13.2
), and recalling the definition of
\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{a}}
in (
12.29
), we obtain

(14.1)

\log\frac{\mathsf{L}_{N}(s)}{\mathsf{L}_{N}(S)}=\sum_{k=\lceil N(\mathsf{a}+\epsilon)\rceil}^{\infty}\log\frac{1+\ee^{-t(k-N\mathsf{a})-S}}{1+\ee^{-t(k-N\mathsf{a})-s}}-\mathsf{S}(S,s)-\mathsf{R}(S,s)+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta N}),\quad N\to\infty,

and this identity is valid for
s,S
in any of the regimes from Assumptions
2.7
. To bound the sum involving logs, observe that
t(k+N\mathsf{a})+s>0
whenever
s
is in any of the regimes from Assumptions
2.7
. Using the basic inequalities
0\leq\log(1+u)\leq u
, which are valid for
u\geq 0
, we obtain

\sum_{k=\lceil N(\mathsf{a}+\epsilon)\rceil}^{\infty}\log\left(1+\ee^{-t(k-N\mathsf{a})-s}\right)\leq\sum_{k=\lceil N(\mathsf{a}+\epsilon)\rceil}^{\infty}\ee^{-t(k-N\mathsf{a})-s}=\frac{\ee^{-t(\lceil N(\mathsf{a}+\epsilon)\rceil-N\mathsf{a})-s}}{1+\ee^{-t}}\leq\frac{\ee^{-Nt\epsilon-s}}{1+\ee^{-t}}.

This shows that the sum of logs above is
\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta N})
. Obviously, we may replace
s
by
S
in the argument just performed. As a consequence we update (
14.1
) to

\log\frac{\mathsf{L}_{N}(s)}{\mathsf{L}_{N}(S)}=-\mathsf{S}(S,s)-\mathsf{R}(S,s)+\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta N}),\quad N\to\infty.

Recall that
\mathsf{S}(S,s)
is as in (
13.33
). In that expression, notice that
N\mathcal{X}^{\scriptscriptstyle({\scriptscriptstyle\rm in})}=\mathbb{Z}\cap(N(\mathsf{a}-\epsilon),N(\mathsf{a}+\epsilon))
(see (
12.35
)), and the explicit term in the right-hand side of (
2.35
) coincides with
-\mathsf{S}(S,s)
with the identification
\mathsf{H}_{N}=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\mathcal{H}_{N}
. The proof of Theorem
2.8
is now completed directly from Theorem
9.2
and Proposition
13.5
.

14.2.
Proof of Theorem
2.9

To prove Theorem
2.9
, we first establish the following result.

Theorem 14.1
.

Under the same hypotheses of Theorem
2.8
,

(i)

Subcritical regime:
There exists
\eta>0
such that for
s,S
in the subcritical regime, with
s<S
, the estimate

(14.2)

\log\frac{\mathsf{L}_{N}(s)}{\mathsf{L}_{N}(S)}=-\left(1+\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-\eta s/N^{1/3}}+N^{-\nu}\right)\right)\int_{\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{V}}s/(tN^{1/3})}^{\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{V}}S/(tN^{1/3})}\mathsf{A}(y,y)\,\mathrm{d}y,\quad N\to\infty,

is valid for any
\nu\in(0,1/6)
.

(ii)

Critical regime:
For
s,S
in the critical regime, with
s<S
, the estimate

(14.3)

\log\frac{\mathsf{L}_{N}(s)}{\mathsf{L}_{N}(S)}=-\left(1+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\nu})\right)\int_{\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{V}}s/(tN^{1/3})}^{\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{V}}S/(tN^{1/3})}\mathsf{K}_{\rm P34}(0,0{\,|\,}y)\,\mathrm{d}y,\quad N\to\infty,

is valid for any
\nu\in(0,1/3)
.

(iii)

Supercritical regime:
For
s,S
in the supercritical regime, with
s<S
, the estimate

(14.4)

\log\frac{\mathsf{L}_{N}(s)}{\mathsf{L}_{N}(S)}=-\frac{\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{V}}^{3}}{12t^{3}N}\left(S^{3}-s^{3}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{S^{4}-s^{4}}{N^{3/2}}\right),\quad N\to\infty,

is valid.

Proof.

Let
S>s
be given. Using (
2.31
), (
2.29
) and (
13.2
), we express

\log\frac{\mathsf{L}_{N}(s)}{\mathsf{L}_{N}(S)}=-\mathsf{S}(S,s)+\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{a}}}\log\frac{\sigma(x{\,|\,}s)}{\sigma(x{\,|\,}S)}-\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\log\frac{1+\ee^{-t(k-N\mathsf{a})-s}}{1+\ee^{-t(k-N\mathsf{a})-S}}-\mathsf{R}(S,s)+\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-\eta N}\right),\quad N\to\infty.

From the very definition of
\sigma
and
\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{a}}
in (
10.3
) and (
12.29
), the two sums combine into the single sum

\sum_{k=\lceil N(\mathsf{a}+\epsilon)\rceil}^{\infty}\log\frac{1+\ee^{-t(k-N\mathsf{a})-s}}{1+\ee^{-t(k-N\mathsf{a})-S}},

and for
s,S
in any of the regiems from Assumptions
2.7
standard arguments show that this sum is
\mathcal{O}(\ee^{-\eta N})
, for some
\eta>0
. Therefore

\log\frac{\mathsf{L}_{N}(s)}{\mathsf{L}_{N}(S)}=-\mathsf{S}(S,s)-\mathsf{R}(S,s)+\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-\eta N}\right),\quad N\to\infty,

for some
\eta>0
, uniformly for
s,S
in any of the regimes from Assumptions
2.7
. The proof now follows directly from the estimates for
\mathsf{R}
and
\mathsf{S}
provided by Proposition
13.5
and Theorems
13.8
,
13.9
and
13.10
.
∎

We are now ready to prove Theorem
2.9
.

Start first with
s
in the subcritical regime, and choose
\delta>0
such that
S\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\delta N^{1/2}>s
. Applying Theorem
14.1
–(i) and using (
13.45
), we obtain

\mathsf{L}_{N}(s)=\mathsf{L}_{N}(\delta N^{1/2})-\left(1+\mathcal{O}\left(\ee^{-\eta s/N^{1/3}}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(N^{-\nu}\right)\right)\int_{\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{V}}s/(tN^{1/3})}^{\infty}\mathsf{A}(y,y)\mathrm{d}y.

The term
\mathsf{L}_{N}(\delta N^{1/2})
is now estimated by Corollary
5.5
, which concludes the proof of Theorem
2.9
–(i).

Next, assume that
s
is in the critical regime. Choose
M>0
such that
s<S\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=MN^{1/3}
, and make sure also that
S
belongs to both the sub and critical regimes. Asymptotics (
1.22
) ensures
\mathsf{K}_{{\rm P34}}(0,0{\,|\,}y)
decays exponentially fast as
y\to+\infty
, and from Theorem
14.1
–(ii) we obtain

\mathsf{L}_{N}(s)=\mathsf{L}_{N}(MN^{1/3})-\left(1-\mathcal{O}\left(N^{-\nu}\right)\right)\int_{\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{V}}s/(tN^{1/3})}^{\infty}\mathsf{K}_{{\rm P34}}(0,0{\,|\,}y)\mathrm{d}y,\quad N\to\infty.

We now apply Theorem
2.9
–(i) to the term
\mathsf{L}_{N}(MN^{1/3})
, which ensures that it decays exponentially fast as
N\to\infty
, and therefore it can be suppressed as an error term in the above, concluding the proof of Theorem
2.9
–(ii).

Assume at last that
s
is in the supercritical regime. Choose
M>0
ensuring that
S\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=-MN^{1/3}>s
and that it is in both the critical and supercritical regimes. This way, the integral

\int_{\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{V}}S/(tN^{1/3})}^{\infty}\mathsf{K}_{\rm P34}(0,0{\,|\,}y)\mathrm{d}y=\int_{-\mathsf{c}_{\mathsf{V}}M/t}^{\infty}\mathsf{K}_{\rm P34}(0,0{\,|\,}y)\mathrm{d}y

is finite, and Theorem
2.9
–(ii) then ensures that
\mathsf{L}_{N}(-MN^{1/3})
is
\mathcal{O}(1)
. Theorem
2.9
–(iii) then follows from Theorem
14.1
–(iii).

Appendix A
A useful lemma

The following lemma is extremely simple, but it turned out out to be useful in certain steps in the main text.

Lemma A.1
.

Fix
r>0
. There exists
M=M(r)>0
such that

\left|\frac{1}{1-w}-1\right|\leq M|w|\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{1}{|1-w|}\leq\frac{M}{|w|},

for every
w\in\mathbb{C}
which satisfies

|1-w|\geq r.

Proof.

For the first claimed inequality, simply write

\left|\frac{1}{1-w}-1\right|=\left|\frac{w}{1-w}\right|\leq\frac{1}{r}|w|.

The second claimed inequality is equivalent to showing that

\left|\frac{w}{1-w}\right|\leq M.

Suppose first that
|w|\leq 2
. Then this inequality is trivially satisfied for
M=2/r
.

Suppose now that
|w|\geq 2
. We write

\left|\frac{w}{1-w}\right|=\frac{1}{|1-w^{-1}|},

and we bound the denominator in the right-hand side as follows

\left|1-\frac{1}{w}\right|\geq 1-\frac{1}{|w|}\geq\frac{1}{2}.

Hence, the final result follows with the choice
M=\max\{2/r,2\}
.
∎

Appendix B
Residue conditions in discrete RHPs

In the course of the paper, we came across several RHPs with residue conditions. We now book-keep a lemma which was useful in transiting between different formulations of residue conditions that appeared.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
\mathbf{X}
be an
m\times m
matrix-valued function with a simple pole at a point
p\in\mathbb{C}
and analytic elsewhere near
p
, and let
\mathbf{R}
be a fixed matrix with
\mathbf{R}^{2}=0
. Furthermore, let
\zeta=\varphi(z)
be a conformal map from a neighborhood of the point
p
to a neighborhood of a point
q=\varphi(p)
. Set
\mathbf{\Phi}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbf{X}(z)
, which has a simple pole at
\zeta=q
and it is analytic elsewhere near
q
.

The following statements are equivalent.

(i)

The product
\mathbf{X}(z)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{1}{z-p}\mathbf{R}\right)
is analytic at
z=p
.

(ii)

\displaystyle{\mathop{\rm Res}_{z=p}\,\mathbf{X}(z)=\lim_{z\to p}\mathbf{X}(z)\mathbf{R}}
.

(iii)

The product
\mathbf{\Phi}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\varphi^{\prime}(p)}{\zeta-q}\mathbf{R}\right)
is analytic at
\zeta=q
.

(iv)

\displaystyle{\mathop{\rm Res}_{\zeta=q}\;\mathbf{\Phi}(\zeta)=\varphi^{\prime}(p)\lim_{\zeta\to q}\mathbf{\Phi}(z)\mathbf{R}}
.

Proof.

The equivalence of (i)–(ii) is essentially in
[
23
, Lemma 4.4]
, see also
[
8
, Lemma 7.1]
where the equivalence statement is explicitly written. We reproduce the proof from
[
8
]
because it will be instructive for the other parts.

Without loss of generality we assume
p=0
. Write

(B.1)

\mathbf{X}(z)=\frac{\mathbf{X}_{-1}}{z}+\mathbf{X}_{0}+\mathcal{O}(z),\quad z\to 0.

A direct expansion shows that

\mathbf{X}(z)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{1}{z-p}\mathbf{R}\right)=-\frac{\mathbf{X}_{-1}\mathbf{R}}{z^{2}}+\frac{\mathbf{X}_{-1}-\mathbf{X}_{0}\mathbf{R}}{z}+\mathcal{O}(1),\quad z\to 0.

Hence, (ii) is equivalent of saying that
\mathbf{X}_{-1}\mathbf{R}=0
and
\mathbf{X}_{-1}=\mathbf{X}_{0}\mathbf{R}
. Under the condition
\mathbf{R}^{2}=0
, these two equalities are equivalent to the single equality
\mathbf{X}_{-1}=\mathbf{X}_{0}\mathbf{R}
, which in turn is equivalent to (i).

Next, the equivalence between (iii) and (iv) is the same as the equivalence between (i) and (ii). Consequently, once we prove that (ii) implies (iv), then we get that (iii) implies (i) and all equivalences are established.

To prove that (ii) implies (iv), we compute from (
B.1
) (already using (i) as well)

\mathbf{\Phi}(\zeta)=\frac{\mathbf{X}_{0}\mathbf{R}}{\varphi^{-1}(z)}+\mathbf{X}_{0}+\mathcal{O}(\zeta-q),\quad\zeta\to q.

Therefore, using that
\mathbf{R}^{2}
,

\mathbf{\Phi}(\zeta)\left(\mathbf{I}-\frac{\varphi^{\prime}(0)}{\zeta-q}\mathbf{R}\right)=\left(\frac{1}{\varphi^{-1}(\zeta)}-\frac{\varphi^{\prime}(0)}{\zeta-q}\right)\mathbf{X}_{0}\mathbf{R}+\mathcal{O}(1),\quad\zeta\to q.

The result now follows from the calculation

\varphi^{-1}(\zeta)=\frac{1}{\varphi^{\prime}(0)}(\zeta-q)(1+\mathcal{O}(\zeta-q)),\;\zeta\to q,\quad\text{which implies}\quad\frac{1}{\varphi^{-1}(\zeta)}-\frac{\varphi^{\prime}(0)}{\zeta-q}=\mathcal{O}(1),\quad\zeta\to q.

∎

Appendix C
The Airy parametrix

With
\ai(\zeta)
being the Airy function, introduce

(C.1)

{\mathbf{Ai}}_{0}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sqrt{2\pi}\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}/12}\begin{pmatrix}\ai(\zeta)&\ai(\ee^{4\pi\mathrm{i}/3}\zeta)\\
\ai^{\prime}(\zeta)&\ee^{4\pi\mathrm{i}/3}\ai^{\prime}(\ee^{4\pi\mathrm{i}/3}\zeta)\end{pmatrix}\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\bm{\sigma}_{3}/6},

which satisfies
\det{\mathbf{Ai}}_{0}(\zeta)\equiv 1
, and assemble

(C.2)

{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\ee^{\pi\mathrm{i}\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}{\mathbf{Ai}}_{0}(\zeta)\times\begin{cases}\mathbf{I},&0<\arg\zeta<\frac{2\pi}{3},\\
(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{E}_{21}),&\frac{2\pi}{3}<\arg\zeta<\pi,\\
(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{E}_{12}),&-\frac{2\pi}{3}<\arg\zeta<0,\\
(\mathbf{I}-\mathbf{E}_{12})(\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{E}_{21}),&-\pi<\arg\zeta<-\frac{2\pi}{3}.\end{cases}

With

\Gamma_{\mathbf{Ai}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Gamma_{0}^{0}\cup\Gamma_{2}^{0}\cup\Gamma_{3}^{0}\cup\Gamma_{4}^{0},

the matrix
{\mathbf{A}}
is introduced so as to solve the following RHP.

RHP C.1
.

Find a
2\times 2
matrix-valued function
{\mathbf{Ai}}
with the following properties.

(1)

{\mathbf{Ai}}
is analytic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus\Gamma_{{\mathbf{Ai}}}
.

(2)

The matrix
{\mathbf{Ai}}
has continuous boundary values
{\mathbf{Ai}}_{\pm}
along
\Gamma_{{\mathbf{Ai}}}
, and they are related by
{{\mathbf{Ai}}}_{+}(\zeta)={{\mathbf{Ai}}}_{-}(\zeta)\mathbf{J}_{{{\mathbf{Ai}}}}(\zeta)
,
\zeta\in\Gamma_{{\mathbf{Ai}}}
, with

(C.3)

\mathbf{J}_{{\mathbf{Ai}}}(\zeta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{cases}\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{E}_{12},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{0}^{0}=(0,+\infty),\\
\mathbf{I}+\mathbf{E}_{21},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{2}^{0}\cup\Gamma_{4}^{0},\\
\mathbf{E}_{12}-\mathbf{E}_{21},&\zeta\in\Gamma_{3}^{0}=(-\infty,0).\end{cases}

(3)

As
\zeta\to\infty
,

(C.4)

{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)=\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}(\zeta^{-1})\right)\zeta^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{U}_{0}\ee^{-\frac{2}{3}\zeta^{3/2}\bm{\sigma}_{3}}.

(4)

The matrix
{\mathbf{Ai}}
remains bounded as
\zeta\to 0
.

To our knowledge, this RHP was first considered in
[
43
]
, where they also showed that the solution is precisely given by
{\mathbf{Ai}}
as defined in (
C.1
)–(
C.2
), see
[
43
, Lemma 7.1 and Equation (7.30)]
3
3

3

Our construction of
{\mathbf{Ai}}
matches the matrix
\Psi^{\sigma}
from
[
43
, Equation (7.9)]
through the simple transformation
\Psi^{\sigma}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\ee^{\pi\mathrm{i}/12}\ee^{-\pi\mathrm{i}\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}{\mathbf{Ai}}
.
. For the record, the asymptotic condition (
C.4
) can alternatively be expressed as

{\mathbf{Ai}}(\zeta)=\zeta^{-\bm{\sigma}_{3}/4}\mathbf{U}_{0}\left(\mathbf{I}+\mathcal{O}(\zeta^{-3/2})\right)\ee^{-\frac{2}{3}\zeta^{3/2}\bm{\sigma}_{3}},\quad\zeta\to\infty.
